Power swelled through Kara as she looked out onto the desk of her new ship, grasp firm as she squared her shoulders and spread her feet.

Kara’s cheeks hurt from how much she was smiling, but she couldn’t possibly stop herself as she glanced back at Lena’s fleet to see them starting to move. As if Lena could feel her gaze, The Oryx’s Revenge gave a brief lurch forward as the current started to push them to move.

Silently thanking her lover, Kara turned back with a deep breath. The ship was set to sail, the wind catching the black sails perfectly. The ship had been positioned parallel to the shore, so Kara threw the wheel to spin starboard, sharply turning out towards sea.

The feeling of the ship moving, the sound of the wheel turning, the smell of the breeze that blew across the desk… It was all so nostalgic, as if it was Kara’s first time on a ship.

She stopped the spinning wheel with a powerful hand as soon as they turned into position, straightening it as she looked to the right to see Lena’s fleet sailing a few hundred yards away.

The Devil’s Heart led the fleet, The Oryx’s Revenge sailing next to a crimson red ship named Burning Rose, Kara unsure if she should pull next to Lena’s ship. After about twenty minutes Kara noticed that she was starting to pick up speed, sailing passed the Burning Rose, towards the front of the fleet with The Devil’s Heart.

A hawk simultaneously flew down onto the deck of Kara’s ship, landing on the messenger perch built on the helm’s deck. Stepping away from the wheel, Kara pulled a small scroll from the hawk’s leg, instantly recognizing Lena’s curling hand writing when she unrolled it.

”Your place is next to me, Captain Danvers. Always and forever. –Athena”

Kara smiled, her heart swelling as she tucked the message into her pocket. She looked up to see The Oryx’s Revenge pulling up right next to The Devil’s Heart, close enough that she could see her crew down below. While her ship had more decks, making it taller, Lena’s ship was considerably longer by at least three cannon columns.

Moving closer to the banister that ran along the entire ship, Kara rested her hands on the wide wood to see Lena maybe ten yards away doing the exact same. The smile that had only just faded from Kara’s face split across her cheeks once again as she looked at the love of her life.

She pulled the message from her pocket, gesturing to it. “I’ll keep that in mind for the future, then,” Kara called loudly across the waves between them. She couldn’t exactly hear Lena laugh- maybe just a little over the wind –but she could see her toss her head back, teeth flashing brilliantly.

They happily looked at one another for a long moment before Kara nodded and turned back to check on her crew. She glanced over her shoulder one more time to see Lena’s lingering gaze before she also turned away.

**********

Kara had briefly left the helm, asking the same woman who gave her the hat- her name was Talia –to just steer them in case they fell off course. She stepped into her captain’s quarters, sitting at her new desk to try and get her bearings of her new office. She noticed that the maps that Lena had provided her that laid across the desk did in fact show the island in the lower left corner.

She was studying the maps when she noticed that there was a quick decrease in speed, the wind no longer howling against the windows in back of her. Looking up in surprise, Kara quickly stood and put her hat on before striding out of her quarters.

They had been traveling for quite some time- at least five hours –so there was no land to mark where they were. Kara glanced up to Talia, who looked just as confused as her. She was just about to go up and question the crew member when she noticed that The Devil’s Heart was pulling up incredibly close to them.

Quickly Kara commanded for the sails to be drawn up so that they matched speed with Lena’s ship, walking to the starboard side to see the opposite crew coming up with a gangplank. Once their speeds were perfectly matched they connected the two with ropes and the plank.

Lena crossed over the plank casually, dropping down onto some make-ship steps made of crates onto Kara’s deck. “What’s up?” She asked her raven haired lover, meeting her right as she stepped onto the deck.

“We’re at the spot we first met,” Lena explained, almost perfectly veiled anxiety flashing in her eyes. Kara turned to look port side, unable to recognize anything. “Were we really that close to the island?” She quirked an eyebrow, crossing the deck with Lena to look out into the sea.

She tried to look down into the water to see if she couldn’t see any of the remains of the battle down at the bottom, but the wreckage must have been too deep. “How do you know? Do the souls of the dead call out to your or something?” Kara tried to sound casual, even though she felt her own anxiety at the knowledge of where they were settled in.

“That’s an oddly accurate guess,” Lena mumbled, quirking her eyebrow. Kara awkwardly laughed in surprise, turning to lean against the railing of the ship. “I guess we should say a few words?” She looked down into the waves, still unable to see the wreckage due to the depth of the water.

Lena moved closer, shifting into a wide stance. “I’ve got an idea, actually.” She took a deep breath, holding her hand out to the ocean with her palm down. She started to move her hand in a sort of beckoning motion, drawing something out of the water with following movements.

Slowly, something started to ebb out of the water. With each pull of Lena’s hand, the massive object drew closer and closer to the surface until finally the tip of the mast broke through the waves. It took Kara only a brief second to realize that Lena was pulling the wreckage of The Oryx out of the water, watching it slowly appear.

It took only a few minutes before the blue and red vessel was resting on the surface like some sort of ghost. Two of the three masts were snapped or missing half of its top completely. Seaweed hung from the sides, and Kara could tell it was laying on its port side with how the barnacles were starting to form along the hull of the ship.

Lena’s face twitched in concentration as she pulled The Oryx right up next to its name sake, her fingers moving in odd ways. The crew of The Oryx’s Revenge all stared in awe at the ghost-ship, only a few snapping out of it when they realized Lena meant for them to bind the wreckage so Kara and her could board her.

With the ropes and planks holding the ship still, all Lena had to focus on was keeping her wresting on the surface. “Would you like to go on first?” Lena asked, her hand resting against her side. Kara looked from the water-logged deck to her lover, a stoic façade hiding her emotions from everyone but Lena.

Wordlessly she nodded, stepping across the small gap using the plank.

As soon as her boots hit the soft wood of The Oryx it felt as if a cold wind was biting straight to the bone. She shivered, blue eyes scanning around the ship she once called her own.

In the background Lena told Kara’s crew to give them some privacy before she also crossed over onto The Oryx. She landed softly behind Kara, green eyes watching her lover for a long moment.

At first Kara refused to move, her hands tight fists at her side. She could see the damage of her ship; the broken planks, the holes of cannon balls, the rusty cannons and sea kelp strewn across the deck… Emotion welled in her chest as she swallowed hard.

As if walking with weighted boots, Kara took a shuffling step forward, then the next.

Behind her Lena looked away from her tense back to glance around the ship. “Are you sure you want me here?” She asked carefully, feeling awkwardly stiff.

Kara turned to look at Lena- the one she should be mad at, even though all she felt for her was how much she loved the pirate captain –silently extending a hand to her lover.

Carefully Lena moved forward to take the hand, frowning.

“I’m glad you’re here… I want to show you my life before I met you; who I was…” She squeezed Lena’s hand, slowly starting to move towards the stern of the ship, where her captain’s quarters were.

As she walked she felt like the souls of her crew were walking with her.

Winn’s hand brushed gently against the one moving at her side. She glanced over to him, grinning at his goofy smile. “A chick, huh?... No wonder we never got together, Captain,” Winn’s voice echoed humorously through the wind.

Quietly scoffing through her nose, Kara looked back ahead as she moved to push the dripping wet door of her quarters open. She pulled Lena into the dark room, Winn following along behind them.

Even though everything was pretty much displaced or washed away, Kara could easily make out the skeletal remains of what once was her office. She quietly explained where everything once was to Lena, her voice dripping with emotion.

She felt the sadness at her loss of ship and crew, but the reminder of her new life- a happier life –kept her from feeling that bitter anger that she was consumed in so many months ago.

Lena seemed stiff and awkward as she looked around, eyebrows pulled tightly together. Winn circled around her, looking at Kara with his usual grin over Lena’s shoulder. “She seems like a keeper… You look at her as if she’s your entire universe.”

’she is,’ Kara thought, glancing from Winn to Lena.

Her lover was looking at probably one of the only paintings that wasn’t washed off the wall. It was horribly damaged, with thick green algae covering most of it. Through the slime she was just able to make out Kara and Winn’s face.

“It was my first portrait as a captain with Winn, my second mate,” Kara explained, moving to stand behind Lena. She looked stoic in the picture, her captains uniform just visible under the algae. Winn had a small smirk pulling at his lips, humor dazzling in his eyes.

Kara remembered when they got that portrait painted; how long it took- how hard it was not to crack jokes with Winn constantly. “I complained for days afterwards about how much my face hurt from having to smile for three days straight… You told me it was my fault for insisting I smirk through the entire process,” Winn’s windy voice spoke from behind Kara, dripping with humor.

Lena stared at the painting for a long while, her face unreadable. “I… I feel so selfish,” Lena mumbled, turning to face her lover. Kara gave her a bewildered look, surprised to see her eyes glassy. “What- why?!”

She stepped in close to hug Lena, strong arms holding her tight. “Because I took you away from all of this… From your best friend… A-And now I’m making you comfort me- I should be comforting you!”

Kara couldn’t help but laugh, the sound coming low from her chest.

Now it was Lena’s turn to look confused, leaning away to look at her girlfriend as if she was crazy. “You seem to forget I’ve been with you willingly for quite some time now, Lena. You’re not forcing me into this relationship- into loving you… Our past is full of blood and very negative feelings, I understand that, but what that blood shed has led to is more than worth it.”

Still frowning, Lena looked up at Kara, guilt continuing to drip from her features. “I’ve said it before, and I’ll say it again; you didn’t know who I was, or who I was going to be when you attacked my ship all those months ago… We can’t blame you for simply doing what this curse has been driving you to do all this time.”

Lena looked down at her boots, eyebrows furrowed. “I still want to do something to at least try and make this right,” she mumbled to the ground.

Gratitude for Lena poured through Kara as she watched her, smiling thoughtfully. She knew all of this emotion was probably new to her after feeling nothing for such a long time, and it was probably incredibly scary.

“What would you like to do?” Kara asked softly, her thumb rubbing her girlfriends side reassuringly. “Can I say a few words? To Winn, and everyone else?”

Kara was nodding before the question was even completely out of Lena’s mouth, letting her pull away to look around the room. She took a deep breath, trying to figure out what to say first.

Moving back, she drew her sword, speaking as if she was talking to a crowd. “I know I can’t give back the soul’s I’ve stolen with this sword,” She moved to lay the sword flat in both hands, “and I know I can’t take back the pain I’ve inflicted on those around me...”

Winn stood in front of Lena, watching her with a curious gaze. Kara had never seen him so serious, his blue eyes boring down at the sword that had once been inside his chest.

“…But I can at least start repaying your souls by expressing my deepest sorrows, and apologize for the cruelties I’ve done... Winn,” He looked up at the sound of his voice, looking deep into Lena’s face as she spoke to the room, “I might not have known you, but I love the person who considered you family…”

He glanced over to Kara, who watched Lena with affectionate eyes. “…You sounded like a good man, and I thank you for making Kara happy when I wasn’t there… I’m sorry for ending such an innocent life so soon, and I’m even more sorry that I can’t bring that life back…”

Lena looked up from her sword, looking directly at Winn, as if she could see him. “…To make up for your death, I vow to protect the one we both love with all our hearts. I’ll make sure someone like me never comes and steals her away like I stole you.”

The smile on Winn’s face was probably one of the largest Kara had ever seen. He moved forward and placed both hands over Lena’s, on top of the sword. She looked down, seemingly feeling the cool touch against her palms.

“I forgive you,” Winn’s voice whispered through the entire room, both women’s hairs fluttering at the sound.

Moving forward to place a gentle palm on the small of Lena’s back, Kara held Winn’s gaze with a thankful look.

“Winn,” Kara’s voice was full of emotion as she spoke, “I’m so glad you came into my life. You were like a brother to me, even when you were driving me crazy with your goofy ways most days... I’ve thought of you every day since you left- and I’ll think of you every day after this. I hope you can be as happy as I am now.”

Winn laughed joyfully, his eyes glittering with amusement much like his portrait. “Go on, Kara… Live your life; for me.” He took his hands off of Lena’s and backed away.

Both Kara and Lena watched him as he disappeared into the darkness silently, the sound of the waves crashing against the broken hull of The Oryx the only noise echoing through the room.

They stood like that for a long time, a feeling of satisfied acceptance pouring over Kara like warm water.

She sighed, shifting to look at her lover as Lena put her saber back at her side. “Thank you for saying something. It means a lot, and I know Winn approved.”

The guilty weight that had been pushing at Lena’s shoulders seemed to lessen. She stood up straighter, her shoulders squaring as she looked to Kara with a small smile. “You know,” She mumbled thoughtfully, “I forgot what it felt like to feel things like sadness and mourning. This is was a well-needed refresher.”

Kara smiled, leaning down to briefly kiss her lover’s lips.

“Yeah, this helped me, too,” She whispered against the kiss, bathing in the warm reminder that while she had lost someone she loved, she gained someone she loved just as much- if not more.

They hung around the ghost of The Oryx for a little longer, Kara taking her to show the parts of the ship that hadn’t been blown or washed away. Lena listened with interest, laughing along to some of the reminiscing stories her girlfriend told her.

Eventually they crossed back over to The Oryx’s Revenge, Kara looking back at her old ship; her old life.

She was content with where she was now, even if it had a bitter start.

Even with the pain and anger of losing her old life, Kara wouldn’t change a thing if it meant she could be with Lena.

The entire crew watched as The Oryx slowly sunk down into the depths once again. Kara watched it with a thoughtful look, her heart sad but accepting of the changing times. Lena stood next to her, silently glancing between the falling ship and Kara.

Once the tip of the center mast was well hidden by the deep ocean blue, she finally turned away from her old life, looking onto her new one.

With a deep, cleansing breath, Kara started to walk Lena back over to The Devil’s Heart. Before she walked up onto the gangplank, Lena turned to her lover. “You’re certain everything is okay?” She raised an eyebrow, green eyes studying her face.

Kara smiled, leaning forward to sweetly kiss Lena.

“All my demons have been slayed… Now let’s go kill yours.”

Hey, Sister

Chapter Summary

Kara meets someone unexpected.

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Kara strolled across the gangplank, landing heavily on the deck of The Devil’s Heart for the first time since she’d been captured. She glanced around the ship that had once been a bad omen to her; a message of death and destruction.

Now, it was just the ship of her lover; a powerful vessel splitting the waves with unnatural ease. There were no more bad memories tied to the painted black deck, or the crimson sails. She felt at ease walking across the creaking wood towards the helm.

Lena stood powerfully, hand resting casually on the wheel as she kept the ship close to The Oryx’s Revenge. Kara trotted up the steep stairs, removing her captain’s hat as she moved closer; it was an unspoken rule that while on The Devil’s Heart she wasn’t the captain, but instead the first mate.

Seeing her blonde lover, Lena smiled wide. “You called, Captain?” Kara grinned back, resting her hands behind her back. Lena adjusted her own captains hat, pushing the wide brim up as the wind fluttered against her dark red and grey jacket.

“I did… I have some thoughts, so I’ve called everyone here to discuss it before we get any closer to the main land,” Lena explained, casually stepping away from the helm. She requested Kara to follow her, trotting down to the deck of the ship before turning into her quarters.

Unlike Kara’s office and bedroom, Lena only had one level, but the room was considerably larger. A large bed was set into one of the walls, privacy curtains pulled aside. The bed was clearly unused, the sheets clean and with very little wrinkling.

Lena had a typically large desk pushed farther back into the room, covered in maps and charting tools. However, in the center of the room was a massive table with at least ten chairs. A beautiful candle chandelier hung above the table, lighting the entire room.

Much like her room back at National City, almost every wall was covered in book shelves. Whatever area didn’t have shelves either had impersonal paintings, or nothing at all.

Kara had realized long ago that Lena never really got attached to material objects, considering her dangerous life style and the constant threat of death. Instead she cared more about stories and things she could fondly keep in her head.

No one else had arrived yet, the room empty. Lena led Kara to the head of the table, instructing her to sit to her right; the first mates seat.

As Kara sat, Lena leaned to pour her a small glass of wine- something she’d never do for anyone but her lover, Kara was well aware.

“So, have you picked a first mate yet?” She asked casually, sliding the cup over to Kara, who softly thanked her. “No, not yet… There’s a few of my crew who I’m already getting close with, but I’m still figuring them out.” Lena sat down after putting the wine back in the center of the table, eyes watching Kara as she spoke.

“You’ve been captain for less than forty-eight hours, that’s to be expected. But they’re treating you okay? No one’s been disrespectful?” Lena gave her girlfriend a serious look, her eyebrow arching.

Kara laughed, her chest swelling with affection at knowing how protective Lena was. “Even if they were giving me a hard time, I’d deal with it myself, Love. I don’t need you running to fix all my problems.”

Lena hummed, looking unconvinced that she didn’t need to protect her constantly. Kara laughed at the look, about to reach over and reassuringly grab her girlfriends hand when suddenly one of the second mates entered.

She greeted Lena first, then Kara, politely nodding with a smile. Dropping the previous subject, Kara talked casually with the second mate, the rest eventually filing in; there was a new second mate who sailed Lena’s newest ship- Demon’s Soul –Kara fondly introducing herself when he sat next to her.

Once everyone was settled in, some grabbing their own wine to sip on, Lena finally started the meeting.

“Thank you everyone for taking a moment to attend… It’s come to my attention that we’ve over looked quite a big detail about this entire plan,” she explained straight away, “there’s no way The Devil’s Heart and the other ships can get anywhere close to the main land.”

Furrowing her eyebrows, Kara sat forward onto her elbows, watching Lena glance around at her mates. It was almost unbelievable that they managed to not notice such a gaping hole in their revenge plot. Of course they wouldn’t be able to get within ten miles of the coast; the navy would be on their asses within minutes.

The Devil’s Heart was too well known- along with the rest of Lena’s fleet –and such a big group of ships would never go unnoticed. Kara was almost worried they might not be able to get within even twenty miles of the shore due to the death of the king in the neighboring country; Azazel did mention that things were tense between the governments.

“So what do you purpose we do?” Leon asked, leaning back in his seat. Lena looked to him, before shifting to look at Kara.

“Your vessel is the only one not tied to my name, Kara- that and Demon’s Soul, with it being new and all. I figured that with just those two ships we could enter the main land,” Lena suggested, the rest of the crew nodding.

However, Kara could already see a tear in the new plan. “With the political tension Azazel mentioned, there’s no way they won’t have doubled the blockade around the land… If it was just my ship passing through, no one would care enough to stop us, but two ships would be noticed.”

Kara looked around, noticing the furrowed eyebrows and confused looks on her crew mate’s faces. “Merchant ships are always escorted by two other vessels,” Kara explained further, “If there was only one armed ship and a merchant, then we’d be stopped; if there were two armed ships but no merchant, we’d also be stopped; if there were two armed ships and a merchant ship, they’d let us through.”

The explanation seemed to click with most, multiple people nodding along with what Kara was saying. “So you’re certain they wouldn’t stop us if we had a merchant with us?” Lena asked, giving her a serious look.

Kara nodded confidently, not shying away from making eye contact. “It’s what I was trained to do when I first became captain. It was a secret guideline we used for stopping ships, so they couldn’t think of secret ways to get around our checks.”

Mentioning her previous life as a naval captain was sort of nerve wracking around a group of pirates, but as Kara glanced around the room she felt a rush of relief realizing there were no judgmental gazes. It was obvious everyone trusted each other in the group, so there was no need to shame her about once being on the ‘wrong side’.

Leaning back in her seat, Lena thoughtfully rested her chin on her fist, her elbow propped on an armrest. “So then we need to commandeer a merchant’s vessel… Will any ship do?”

“It’s not necessarily the shipment or type of vessel we should look at, but instead where they’re going.” Kara paused for a moment, thinking of all the shipping routes she knew of.

Pushing her chair back from the table, Kara quickly grabbed one of the maps from Lena’s desk, spreading it out in front of her. “We’d need to get a ship that’s exporting goods, so that no port under Victoria’s control will raise the alarm that a shipment is missing.”

Lena leaned forward, clasping both hands under her chin as she looked at the maps, realization sparkling in her green eyes. “Oh! That makes sense- it’ll take longer for the neighboring kingdom to notice that their merchant hasn’t showed up, giving us more time to keep the disguise. By the time the warning gets to Victoria we’ll already be on land.”

“Exactly,” Kara grinned, excitement at working together with Lena rushing through her, “So our best option is to pull a ship that’s departing from a trading route farther from the capitol- as well as grabbing them far away from the blockade that’s typically scouting through there.”

Looking at the map, Kara started to point out good routes to pull from. “All of these routes are good” she explained, “However, where I recommend we go- even if it adds a few days onto the trip –is this area, here. Taking a ship from this area here should be easy; they pull from this town over… Here…”

The finger that was gliding across the map, pointing out different trade routes faltered as Kara pointed at the north western town of Midvale. Her eyebrows furrowed, finger curling away from the rough parchment as suddenly a million thoughts ran through her mind.

She’d been so caught up in her excitement about working so well with Lena that she didn’t think to process where the trade route was connecting…

Her home town.

Frowning, Kara stood straight, staring down at the town. Her mother still lived in Midvale last she heard…

It wasn’t the idea of betraying her former home that wrenched Kara’s heart; it was the idea of being so close to her family, yet not being able to see them.

God how badly she wanted to hug her mom and sister one last time- to say a proper goodbye. The last time she’d seen her was well over a year ago. She visited her just before leaving with The Oryx.

It’d been even longer since she’d seen Alex, her sister. She worked farther inland, and with Kara’s job being in the water constantly it was hard to take the time to go see her.

Suddenly realizing that she had gone completely silent, and everyone was staring at her, Kara snapped out of her longing thoughts.

Blinking, she stiffly moved to go sit back down in her seat. “But- uh… Yeah- I recommend we search that area… About thirty miles out from- uhm… Out from the- uh- coast.”

Lena stared at her, concern pooling in green eyes as she furrowed her eyebrows. “Are you okay?” She asked softly, seemingly uncaring that she had completely dropped her Athena façade.

Kara glanced over at her before staring down at the map in front of them.

“I’m fine, I just remembered something is all… Please, continue.” She could feel Lena continuing to stare at her, obviously seeing straight through the lie. Thankfully, however, she turned back to the other mates to continue the meeting.

“Then it’s settled; we’ll detour further up north and capture the first merchant ship we see. It should only add about a week’s travel, and then we’ll return back down south to…” Lena’s voice trailed off into muffled background noise as Kara stopped paying attention.

She leaned against the arm rest of her chair, cheek propped against her palm as she crossed her legs. Kara’s foot bounced absentmindedly in the air as she tried thinking of what her sister looked like.

To her dismay, all she could see was the general parts of her; her deep brown hair, her large brown eyes, the way her jaw was shaped… But the rest seemed to blur the harder Kara tried to think about it. She couldn’t see her lips, or her nose, or her brows…

Of course she wasn’t completely unrecognizable- Kara could still pick her out in a crowed –but it was the fact that she couldn’t remember the small details anymore that upset her so much. It was like forgetting the words to your favorite song after you stopped listening to it for a while.

The sound of multiple chairs scraping against the wooden floor made Kara look up, realizing that Lena had finished the meeting. She also got up, almost knocking her chair over as she clumsily stepped around it.

Refusing to look at Lena, who was very obviously staring at her, Kara started to follow behind with the other mates. She just about reached the door when suddenly Lena’s authoritative voice cut through her like a knife.

“Captain Danvers… A word, if you will.”

Wincing, Kara caught a glimpse of Leon glancing over his shoulder at her, looking worried before the door closed behind him.

Stiffly turning, Kara refused to look directly at her girlfriend. Instead she looked at the scuffed floor under her boots, basically standing at attention.

“Come here,” Lena’s voice coolly commanded, dripping with authority. Kara’s legs moved without her thinking, forcing her over to stand a few feet in front of her terrifying girlfriend.

She still stared at the ground, preparing to hear more irritated words. Instead, all she heard was Lena boots against the floor, closing the distance.

Cool palms slowly slid up Kara’s flushed cheeks, gently guiding her head to tilt up so that they were looking eye to eye.

“What’s wrong, Kara?”

Lena’s gaze was soft and loving, her thumb gently brushing against Kara’s cheek bone as she looked down at her with wide eyes.

“I…” Kara started, but suddenly her mind went completely blank on what to say. She searched Lena’s face, trying to find her words. “…I don’t remember what my sister looks like.”

Kara didn’t realize she was crying until she felt Lena’s thumb wipe away a tear, her expression growing even more worried as she pulled Kara into a tight hug. The embrace only made her cry harder, fists clutching onto her lover’s jacket as if she was a life raft.

She sobbed into Lena’s shoulder, mumbling her explanation about Midvale between sobs. Lena silently listened to her, rubbing her back as she tried to sooth her. She gently pulled Kara across the room, guiding them to the large bed that looked like it was never used.

Helping her into the bed, Lena climbed in with Kara to hold her as she continued to cry. She didn’t complain as Kara pushed her face into her neck and chest, holding her head in her arms as she stroked softly through golden locks.

“Kara, we don’t have to go after that trading route if it’s too painful for you; there’s other options,” Lena softly whispered against her hair, snuggling her closer. Kara sniffled, more hot tears rolling down her cheeks.

“I-I can’t ruin this mission j-just because I’m home s-sick,” Kara mumbled through her hiccupping tears, her eyebrow furrowing. “It’s unfair to get special treatment, just because I’m the captain’s girlfriend… And i-it’s not even the damn town, it’s just that I-I miss my family…”

Lena’s nails gently scratched over Kara’s jacket, moving in a ‘T’ formation between each shoulder, then down her back. She was silent for a long moment, listening to Kara’s slowly fading hiccups as she started to calm.

“I know it hurts, and I know being so close yet not being able to actually getting to see your family is hard… But one day- when this is all over –you’ll be able to see them again; it’ll be complicated to explain, and maybe you’ll have to change some of the detail’s, but they’re not gone forever.”

Moving to lift Kara’s chin up with a bent finger, Lena stared into dewy blue eyes. “I’m sorry you’re hurting, Sweetheart… I wish I could take the pain away…” She bent down and kissed Kara’s sniffling nose, offering her a small, comforting smile.

The affection took the frustration and pain away, allowing Kara to offer a half smile back. She shuffled up a little bit more to softly kiss Lena’s cheek, sighing against cool skin. “You’re right, it’s not the end of the world; I’ll get to see them again… I’m just impatient. Waiting for things to fall into place was never my strong suit.”

Humming softly, Lena pulled Kara back into her chest, hooking their legs together more comfortably. “You are very head-strong when it comes to jumping into action,” she joked, Kara softly snorting through her nose.

“Sorry for getting snot on your jacket,” Kara mumbled, smiling when she heard Lena’s musical laugh vibrating through her chest. It was a comforting sound, that seemed to wipe away Kara’s sadness with ease.

“That’s the least of my worries, Sweetheart,” Lena mused, pausing before adding in a slightly concerned tone, “You’re certain you’re going to be okay? You don’t have to help me with this part of the journey if you don’t want to.”

Suddenly shifting so she was looking up at her lover, Kara gave her a serious look- lightly ruined by her puffy red cheeks and slight sniffling. “Baby, I want to help. I know what I was getting in to when I suggested I join you on this… I’ll be fine, I promise.”

Considering her girlfriend for a long moment, Lena’s protective nature seemed to fade. “If you say so… I’m here if you need to talk about it again, you know that, right?”

Kara smiled up at her, her heart warming in her chest. “I’ll keep that in mind, Love.” She leaned in to kiss Lena softly, her cool skin oddly comforting.

Laying back down, Kara snuggled back into her lover’s chest, happy to feel her arms tightening around broad shoulders. “God, I wish we could stay like this forever,” Kara mumbled into the slightly rough canvas fabric of Lena’s jacket.

They laid like that for almost an hour, enjoying the comforting feeling of each other. Kara was almost about to fall asleep before there was a knock on the door across the room.

Lena sighed, sitting up as Kara shifted to get off of her. “I guess we have to go be captain’s again,” she mumbled with a frown. Kara offered her a reassuring smile.

“As long as you’re by my side, I wouldn’t have it any other way.”

**********

Thankfully the strong winds mixed with Lena’s magical currents sped their journey up north quite a bit. Typically, it’d take Kara a week and some change to get all the way up to Midvale’s trading route, but instead it only took them five days.

They didn’t encounter any other boat along the way, although Kara did spot a few incredibly far off in the distance heading away from them.

Through the five days of travel Kara kept track of where they were meticulously. If she wasn’t sailing, then she was charting. Every once and a while Lena would pull up next to her to visit, or vice versa, but both women were so busy fulfilling their duties as captain that it was hard to spend time together.

Kara was currently looking across the waves at The Devil’s Heart a few hundred yards away, trying to see if she couldn’t spot Lena’s hat from this distance.

She was so busy looking that she almost missed the medium sized ship along her starboard side, just before the horizon. The only reason Kara noticed it was because one of the crew up in the crow’s nest called loudly down to her, pointing it out.

Quickly stepping away from the helm, she pulled out her spyglass to look at the ship sailing towards them, further north. Although they were still quite some ways away, Kara could see that it was clearly a merchant ship.

Smiling at their luck, Kara collapsed the telescope and hurried to write a quick message to Lena, pointing out the ship. She sent the hawk across the water, watching it land safely.

It only took a few minutes before the hawk was flying back over, a reply attached to its legs.

”Good eye, Captain Danvers. You know the plan. –Athena”

As soon as Kara finished reading the message, a bell sounded from The Devil’s Heart, ringing loudly over the whistling wind. There was a noticeable increase in speed as Lena guided them directly towards the targeted vessel.

Bounding across the waves, it took only an hour before they were upon the yellow and navy blue vessel. Lena’s ship blew multiple warning shots dangerously close to the decently sized ship, daring them to try and run.

The flag showed a house crest that Kara didn’t recognize as she steered the ship around to cut off the originally fleeing ship, noticing that Lena was obviously pulling it closer towards her.

The other five ships also circled around the merchant vessel while The Devil’s Heart pulled close to board her; the plan was to keep the ship as undamaged as possible- Lena’s crew would capture or kill the opposing crew and take over.

Kara watched the chaos unfold as she circled close to the rather expensive looking vessel, noticing that lots of its crew were starting to abandon the deck in favor of the water. It didn’t take long for Lena to also notice while she fought on the deck, calling upon the creatures at her disposal to feast upon the fleeing.

Pulling out her telescope again, Kara looked around the merchant deck, seeing what was happening a few hundred yards away. The entire crew consisted of men, Lena up on the helm’s deck fighting off the man who looked to be the captain.

It wasn’t odd that the crew was all men- what was out of the ordinary, however, was when Kara spotted the figure of a woman leaning across the bow of the ship, clearly contemplating if she should jump into the monster infested waters or turn and face the pirates.

Looking closer, Kara felt as if she was staring at a ghost through her spyglass.

Deep brunette hair that stopped just below her chin in a wind-swept bob; large brown eyes noticeable even from the blurry distance of the spyglass; a broad jaw and flat chin that screamed ‘Danvers’.

Alex looked terrified as she looked between the water and the pirates, her lips mouthing a slew of cuss words and god knows what else.

The telescope slipped out of Kara’s hands, clattering loudly to the deck as she stared in disbelief.

The crew standing around her all gave her confused looks as she turned on the heels of her boots and suddenly pulled out of the circling formation she was originally sailing in.

Multiple members frantically asked her what she was doing, but Kara was so focused on steering The Oryx’s Revenge right up close to the merchant ship that she hardly heard them.

Having no time to command her crew to tie them to the vessel on their port side so that she could smoothly board, Kara instead abandoned the wheel to sprint down onto the main deck.

Not slowing her pace Kara took a running jump, grabbing a rope that hung down from the main mast’s sail to swing across the ten-foot distance between her ship and Alex’s.

Kara’s heart was thundering in her chest as she landed heavily on the merchant ship’s deck, rolling with her momentum. She had flung herself into chaos, forcing herself to stand as she looked around wildly.

There was blood and screaming all around her. One merchant crew member rushed at her with what looked to be a pike pole, holding it like a javelin as he gave a wild war-cry. Kara ducked under the pole with ease, simultaneously drawing her sword and slashing through the belly of the man.

Franticly searching for her sister, Kara looked at the bow of the ship just in time to see that Alex had lifted herself up onto the railing of the ship, looking like she was just about to jump.

“ALEX!”

The scream was torn out of her mouth before Kara could think, echoing above the other frantic yelling around the deck.

Hearing the familiar voice, Alex paused and looked back, bewildered at how she could be hearing her dead sister.

Kara shouldered through a group of merchant-men like a football player, barreling past them as she sprinted to reach her sister in time.

One of Lena’s men was also charging for Alex, perpendicular to Kara, his sword held high.

Supernatural strength possessed Kara to push herself to sprint faster, reaching the man before he could harm her sister. She didn’t attack him, instead grabbing him by the scruff of the shirt and yanking him back just before he swung down at Alex’s legs.

The man tumbled to the ground, looking up at Kara as she stood between him and his target. “Not her!” Kara ordered in a booming voice, blue eyes the color of ice as fear gripped her heart.

Baffled, all the man could do was look up at Kara’s terror-driven anger with wide eyes. One of the merchant-men ran at the trio, aiming to swing at the crew member on the ground. Instinctually, Kara blocked the sword blow with her own, parrying the attack and punching the man in the jaw with her free hand.

Recognizing there was nowhere safe on this ship, Kara turned to look her sister in the eye for the first time in over two years, opening her mouth to beckon her down from the edge of the ship.

Terror, confusion, disbelief… So many emotions were swirling in deep brown eyes as Alex looked down at her sister, finally able to see her face as she turned around. She was still standing up on the banister, feet shifting to keep her balance.

“Kara?!”

The sound of hearing Alex’s voice was indescribable; it was like the first rain after months of drought. A rush of relief poured through Kara, but her panic at the situation dulled the sensation.

Panting from the sudden sprint and adrenaline pumping through her veins, Kara reached a hand out to her sister. “Come with me,” she requested, hoping her sister trusted her enough to push past the confusion.

Thankfully Alex jumped back onto the deck, about to reach forward and grab Kara’s extended hand when suddenly another merchant crew member was rushing at Kara.

Having not been paying attention, Kara tensed and moved to raise her sword, realizing she was about to be too late to block the attack.

But no blow came.

Instead, the man froze with a look of agony as his muscles visibly seized. Much like what Kara saw in the woods so many months ago, the man started to sweat and drip water as Lena drained his life force.

Kara looked past the man to see that she was striding towards them with menacing anger etched onto her face, hips swaying in an attractive yet terrifying way. Her hand was extended, fingers twitching as she effortlessly killed the man who attempted to attack her lover.

The fighting seemed to be coming to an end, only a few merchant crew members still attempting to resist as Lena let the husk drop to the ground, green eyes boring into Kara. Through the mask of stoic anger, she could see the fear in the way Lena’s eyebrow twitched.

“Captain Danvers, what in the world are you-“ Lena stared, her voice suddenly cutting off as Alex reached to grab the first weapon she could find; an ore for a row boat- rushing to strike at Lena, who’s glare turned to the older Danvers.

Swiftly moving in front of her sister, Kara managed to grab her around the middle and push her back. “Alex- no!” she scolded, pulling Alex away from Lena.

The raven haired captain tensed when she heard the name of the random woman, green eyes flicking to a rage filled face. “What the hell is going on here?” Lena asked in an authoritative voice.

Alex thrashed against Kara’s tight hold. “What are you doing, Kara?! She’s going to kill us!”

Dropping her thorned saber to grab onto Alex’s shoulders with both hands, Kara shook her sister slightly. “No, she’s not! Alex- stop fighting, let me explain!” Her sister stopped batting at her arms, freezing to look up at her.

Stress and confusion emanated from Alex as she stared at Kara, her mouth opening and closing multiple times as she tried to formulate words. She was clearly in shock, struggling to grasp onto the situation. “Y-You’re supposed to be dead! How are you here? Why are you with that pirate?! What are you wearing?!”

Realizing that she had no idea where to start her story- not that this was currently the right place to be telling the story –Kara looked back at Lena. “Let me take her to my ship, Athena… Please,” she begged, blue eyes pleading.

Lena frowned, glancing away from Kara to look at Alex. “Fine… If you insist.”

Looking out to where The Oryx’s Revenge was slowly drifting past, Lena summoned the currents to pull her in close enough for Kara’s crew to tie her up and put a plank across.

Giving her lover a thankful look, Kara turned back to her frustrated sister. “Alex, you have to trust me… I’ll tell you everything, I swear- but you need to be calm,” she franticly whispered, seeing the stubborn anger in her sister’s eyes.

Glancing past Kara towards Lena, Alex frowned before nodding with a huff. Sagging her shoulders, Kara whispered her thanks under a sigh. She bent and picked up her sword, sheathing it before leading Alex away from the gathering crowd by the elbow.

Lena watched them as they crossed over to The Oryx’s Revenge, a worried frown flashing across her face before she turned away and started to call out commands to her crew.

Once Kara safely guided Alex into her quarters, she let her sister go. Instantly Alex rounded on her and shoved her shoulders.

“What the fuck, Kara?!” Alex yelled, her confusion and fear from earlier returning as she studied her sisters surprised face. “How the fuck are you here?!”

The negative reaction stung against Kara’s heart as she winced at her sister’s anger. She didn’t fight back when Alex shoved her again, stumbling slightly. Tears burned at her eyes, her own frustration building- this was not how she thought this reunion would go.

“I was captured at sea, Alex! Athena took down The Oryx and her fleet, but kept me alive,” Kara tensely replied, her jaw clenching as she struggled not to let her frustrated tears fall.

“Why did she keep you alive- why are you working with her?!”

“It’s a long story-“

“Then you better talk fast, dammit!”

“Alex! Why are you yelling at me?!” Kara’s frustration snapped, her tone bitter as she returned her sister’s glare.

“Because I buried you seven months ago! Because that woman just destroyed my only escape route! Because I thought you were dead, but instead you’re off playing pirate with the bitch who captured you!”

Suddenly Kara was taking a menacing step forward. “Don’t call her a bitch, Alex- you have no idea who she is!” Her words came out in a terrifying growl, Alex’s own anger faltering for a moment.

“I’m not ‘off playing pirate’- I’m trying to save the woman I love from someone I thought I could trust. I didn’t leave you, I was captured and taken to an island where I learned the truth about the woman I used to call my queen, for fucks sake.”

At a loss of words, Alex stared at her sister in disbelief. “What do you mean ‘love’?” Her eyebrows lowered as they furrowed, just like Kara’s did when she was troubled.

Taking in a deep breath, Kara tried to calm her frustration and anxiety, realizing that she was going to have to explain something she might not be ready to explain.

“Can we just… Sit down? I should start from the beginning- to answer all your questions.”

Alex frowned, but eventually nodded. Swallowing the lump in her throat, Kara stiffly moved to her paper-covered desk, gesturing for Alex to sit in one of the chairs. She sat at her own tall-backed chair across the desk, looking at the woman she thought she wouldn’t be seeing for at least a few more years.

Taking a deep breath, Kara clasped her hands on the surface of the desk. “So… To start from the very beginning…”

She explained everything; the death of Winn, the discovery of Lena’s island and city, when she started to learn about the free people and her blossoming feelings for her now girlfriend, as well as the attack on National City and their entire reason for leaving.

She didn’t give away Lena’s real name, nor did she fully explain her curse. Kara simply gave the vague details of her abilities, and that it was Lena’s destiny to kill Victoria. She never mentioned the book of spells, or where exactly the Island was.

“… These people aren’t your enemy, Alex- even if right now they don’t look exactly innocent... I’ve gotten to know them all, and I learned their way of life. Athena’s not the monster Victoria and her government says she is.”

By the end of the story Alex had a stoic face, staring down at one of the blank parchments on Kara’s desk. “So what does the ship I hired have to do with this plan of yours?” She frowned slightly, looking up to meet Kara’s furrowed gaze.

“We need it to get past the blockade, so we can get onto the main land,” Kara explained, watching her sisters frown deepen.

“I’m not-… I can’t go back there, Kara… I refuse to go back home after trying so hard to leave.”

Confusion filled Kara, her natural curiosity easily taking over as she looked at how troubled her sister was. “Why are you running from home?” Her voice was soft and encouraging, seeing her sister’s hesitance.

“After you died- or… Went missing, I guess is a better term… Mom and I were asked to come to the palace, right after your funeral,” Alex explained, her eyes watering at the memory, “We met with the Queen- Victoria… I thought that she was going to give us flag of valor or something, but…”

She squinted her eyes closed, nose scrunching as Alex visibly struggled with the emotions rolling over her. “All she did was interrogate us on if you told us anything before you left… She used some sort of… Power?... I don’t know… She said some weird foreign words, and her hands started to glow… Then I was telling her every little secret I had, without even knowing what I was doing… Mom, too.”

It took a moment for Kara to process Alex’s words, but when she managed to work through the information she felt her heart settle into pure rage. That witch used magic on her family!

If Kara hadn’t already hated Victoria to begin with, she would have most certainly hated her now. Abhorrent loathing filled Kara from deep within; this woman she once considered her leader had not only tried to kill the woman she loved, but had forced some sort of truth spell onto her mother and sister.

“She didn’t hurt you, did she?” Kara growled through clenched teeth, the edges of her vision starting to turn red.

“No, she didn’t hurt us,” Alex answered, watching the resentment rolling off of Kara in waves, “she let us go when she realized we didn’t know anything.”

The relief Kara felt at knowing her family wasn’t tortured on her account was muted by her rage, her blood still boiling.

“But after seeing her… After being used like that, I started to question her leadership,” Alex continued, looking down at her fidgeting hands, “And it didn’t help that when I started to ask my own questions about who exactly Victoria was, I started to get threats from coworkers and anonymous letters.”

Kara’s knuckles were white as she gripped the edges of her armrests. She glared a burning hole into her desk, jaw clenched.

“I’ll make sure you never have to go back there, Alex… I promise you.”

The words seemed to relax Alex, her shoulders slumping as she let all the tension roll off of her. “Thank you, Kara… And uhm…” She picked at a spot on her pants. “I’m really glad you’re not dead…”

Kara’s abhorrence for Victoria screeched to a stop, replaced with the longing feeling to be able to hold her sister in her arms again.

Now that she wasn’t anxiously explaining the past seven months, or full of rage, Kara was able to get a good look at her sister; She had cut her hair since the last time Kara had seen her, and although her face did seem a little more aged, she still looked much younger than her real age.

Standing, Kara walked around her desk to gently wrap her arms around Alex. “I missed you so much… I’m so sorry I couldn’t get to you sooner, but… It’s been a crazy few months.”

The feeling of her sister’s arms wrapping around her tightly sent a wave of happiness through Kara as she buried her face into her shoulder. “I missed you, too,” Alex whispered, voice cracking with emotion.

Neither of them pulled away for a long moment. It wasn’t until someone knocked at the door that Kara stood to her full height. She gave whoever was knocking permission to enter, seeing Talia’s head popping through the slightly open door.

“Captain Danvers?... Captain Athena is moving to board; I thought you might like to prepare a little bit.” She glanced curiously to Alex, who seemed vaguely uncomfortable seeing a ‘pirate’ interacting so casually with Kara.

The blonde captain thanked Talia, nodding her dismissal before turning back to her sister. “If you’d like to go downstairs while I talk to her?” Before the words were even fully out of Kara’s mouth, Alex was shaking her head.

“No- I want to meet this woman; I’ve got a few questions…” She looked determined, that stubborn Danvers expression all over her face. Kara pursed her lips, knowing this probably wasn’t going to be the smoothest introduction; Alex still seemed to not trust Lena, and Lena wasn’t the best at keeping calm when being ‘interrogated’.

Sighing, knowing it was useless to fight her sister, Kara grabbed the hat that she removed earlier and put it on. “Please just… Remember that I care for her,” she pleaded to Alex before turning to walk out to meet Lena.

Right as the door closed behind her, Kara saw her lover crossing the plank onto The Oryx’s Revenge. Her face was hard, eyes narrowed as they scanned the crew, landing sharply on Kara, who quickly walked over.

Stepping heavily onto the deck, Lena stood to her full height as Kara met her with an anxious glimmer in her eye. “I can explain,” Kara reasoned for the hundredth time that day. A muscle twitched in Lena’s jaw, her old walls starting to build up around herself.

“How did she get here, Kara?” Lena questioned, eyes boring into her girlfriends. “Victoria’s wronged her, too, Athena,” the false named felt wrong on Kara’s tongue as she quickly spoke, “She was trying to get away.”

Lena’s perfect brows furrowed, her crimson lips pulling into a thin line. “So you didn’t write a message to her? This just happened by chance?”

Kara stepped in close to her girlfriend, frowning at the mistrust in her voice; she didn’t care that people were obviously staring at their interaction, too focused on quelling her lover’s worries. “I would never do something like that behind your back, my Love- I promise on my heart.”

She spoke low, drawing an ‘X’ over her heart; a vow that both of them took incredibly serious.

Seeing the sacred gesture, Lena’s face softened. Now Kara could see the anxiety in her eyes. “Okay,” Lena sighed the word, “I trust you… My mind just jumped to the worst conclusions-“

Kara interrupted her, grabbing her hand to intertwine their fingers. “I understand, baby,” she basically whispered, pressing their foreheads together, “But I was serious when I promised my loyalty to you… I love you- I would never hurt you like she did.”

Kara didn’t even have to elaborate on who ‘she’ was, Lena instantly understanding what she meant.

Taking a shaky breath, Lena squeezed their clasped hands before admitting, “I really didn’t want to meet your sister like this… Does she hate me?”

Unable to stop her nervous chuckle at Lena’s worries- it was so odd hearing such a powerful woman being so anxious about meeting her girlfriend’s family –Kara gave a small smile, trying to calm her. “Well she’s certainly not a fan, but… Give it time; she’s been through a lot today.”

“Then I could just meet her tomorrow or-“

“No,” Kara insisted, her voice playfully scolding, “We need to rip the Band-Aid off now, or she’s never going to accept you.”

Lena groaned, pulling away to tilt her head back dramatically. She scrunched up her face, clearly not wanting to face her fears.

“Love, you’ve dealt with worse than her; it’ll be fine… Eventually.” Kara smiled at the adorable faces her girlfriend was making.

“You’re not helping, Sweetheart,” Lena grumbled, making a pouty face before quickly replacing it with a stony façade when she noticed someone walking too close.

Kara scoffed, shaking her head as she started to slowly pull Lena towards her office. “It’s now or never, baby… I’ll be right next to you; you don’t have to worry.”

The smile that tugged at crimson lips was a smile that only Kara could ever pull out of Lena, her heart clearly putty in her hands.

“Fine… Let’s go meet your sister, then.”

Chapter End Notes

I think "The Devil's Lettuce" instead of "The Devil's Heart" ONE TIME, and now I can't get it out of my head...

My life is almost as cursed as Lena's... ;P
-Aries

Witch Hunt

Chapter Summary

Kara and Lena's plan falls apart.

Chapter Notes

Thank you for everyone's patience; this was a hard chapter to get right, and I had so many ideas that I didn't know what to chose from. I think I've got the last few chapters planned out properly now, so I'm excited to see how this all goes!

Till next time,
-Aries

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Kara pushed the door to her quarters open, noticing Alex had stood from the desk, and was slowly walking around the office. She turned at the sound of people entering the room, her eyes instantly landing on Lena, who walked in second.

There was a tense silence where Kara glanced between her sister and her lover, watching them stare at one another. Lena’s face was stoic, expertly hiding her anxiety about meeting Alex; even Kara’s trained eye had a hard time seeing the wavering gaze as she struggled to keep her composure.

Alex openly frowned, clearly still disapproving of who exactly Lena was. After a few moments, when it became clear that neither woman knew what to say, Kara spoke first. “Uh… Alex, this is Athena; Athena, this is Alex.” She gestured between the two women, Alex strolling over to stand to Kara’s right.

Awkwardly offering her hand, Lena said something that made both Kara and Alex raise their eyebrow. “Let’s not start this introduction off with false information; you can call me Lena, just like your sister does- although I’d like to keep the name ‘Athena’ around my crew and strangers, if you don’t mind.”

Green eyes glanced to blue, silently searching for her agreement. Kara’s smile was gratifying as she looked to Lena, giving the smallest nod. Alex noticed the look, glancing between the two of them as she gave Lena’s hand a brief shake. “So Athena’s a fake name, then? Why not just go by Lena?”

“The name Athena was given to me by my previous captain when I first joined her crew; a façade to protect my true identity.”

“Did you know Lena’s her real name?” Alex glanced to Kara, who nodded.

“I figured it out shortly after we met, but it took a little while before I actually started to call her that. She also asked me to keep it a secret” Kara remembered how stubborn Lena was in the beginning of their time together; she always got so annoyed when Kara would try and slip ‘Lena’ into her sentences. Now she seems to prefer her real name.

Alex quietly hummed, crossing her arms as she looked back to Lena. “And you’re the one who captured my sister? Took her away to your secret island?” Before Lena could answer, a flash of insult passing through her green eyes, Kara quickly suggested they sit down; this was going to be a long conversation, if Alex kept up with these types of questions.

Stiffly, Lena and Alex sat next to each other, Kara sitting in her usual spot across from the desk. “I did capture your sister when we first met, yes,” Lena explained in a slightly frosty voice, “But to be fair I wasn’t actually going after her, I was going after who she was escorting…”

“She never explained to me why you actually kept her, and didn’t kill her like the rest.”

“Well… It’s a little complicated to explain, but… Originally I held onto Kara because I wanted to use her in my own selfish plot of revenge, but when I subconsciously realized my attraction, I decided to just… Hold onto her.”

“Why not just send her back when you realized you didn’t need her?”

“She’d seen too much, and by that point she’d already been branded by a jealous crew member trying to get rid of her.”

Alex frowned, glancing at Kara at the mention of being branded- Kara only briefly mentioned the brand, brushing over it. Lena eyed the brand under the navy captain’s jacket, clearly remembering the memory with a slight frown.

“And what happened to this crew member who branded my sister?” Alex’s eyes narrowed, as if Lena’s answer was going to truly decide what she thought about her.

“I killed him.”

Lena held the blistering eye contact, not flinching.

All of a sudden Alex relaxed her shoulders, her eyes widening and eyebrows raising in surprise. “You killed one of your own crew? To protect my sister?”

Kara was kind of impressed with her lover; Alex was very well known for her interrogative ways in the Danvers family, yet Lena seemed to be holding her own without Kara having to interject.

Lena confidently nodded, her own cool features softening when she realized Alex’s opinion of her had suddenly shifted.

“I killed multiple members of my own crew that night, for her… I think that’s when Kara’s safety became important to me, compared to when she was just a pawn in my game.”

Kara, who had been silently watching the entire interaction from her chair, smiled. It was kind of heartwarming hearing the exact moment Lena’s fledgling heart started to guide her without Lena even knowing it- even if that moment involved the death of people she once trusted.

Glancing across the desk, Lena noticed the smile. Instantly her own grin broke across her face, visibly melting towards the affection.

“So then you actually care for Kara, then? She’s no longer a ‘pawn’ to you?” Alex’s voice broke through the mini moment, watching the interaction with an unreadable look.

The smile on Lena’s face disappeared into a serious stare as she turned back to Alex; as if what she was about to say, she meant with her life- because she did, in all honesty. “Your sister means absolutely everything to me; without her, I’d still be the blood thirsty monster I was over seven months ago… She’s humanized me- fixed parts of me I didn’t even know were broken. I love your sister more than anything in this world.”

A swell of love and affection filled Kara’s heart as she beamed at her lover. She’d heard Lena tell her that she loves her countless times, but hearing Lena explain and defend their relationship to someone else made her want to cry.

Honestly, if her desk and Alex weren’t in the way, Kara would have been all over Lena, who was returning her loving look.

Alex silently looked between them, pursing her lips and wrinkling her nose. “Please don’t start making out while I’m still in the room…”

Kara snorted, breaking eye contact with Lena to look at her sister. “You’re just jealous,” she mumbled, and- as if a gift from the gods –Alex finally smiled. Relief washed through Kara, seeing her sister’s judging stare finally break.

It seemed as if Alex was content with Lena’s answers to her questions, her relaxed posture remaining as she looked over to Lena. “I don’t normally say this, but; I trust you… If what Kara has told me about you is true, then I’ve no reason not to. Even if she’s in all of this because of you, you’ve clearly taken good care of her, so thank you for that.”

The anxious look that had been constantly swimming behind Lena’s eyes melted away at the words. She grinned thankfully to Alex’s approval, giving her a brief nod. “Of course. I promise to continue taking good care of her, as well.”

“You better- otherwise I’ll kick your ass,” Alex laughed, Kara’s chest swelling with happiness at the sound. God how she missed her sister… And how much she wished her mother was here to meet Lena, too.

Suddenly a thought popped in Kara’s mind; a question she had yet to ask, having been too overwhelmed with everything else. “Why did mom not go with you when you left?”

A sad, yet frustrated look passed over Alex’s face at the mention of their missing mother. She huffed, shaking her head presumably at a memory about Eliza. “She refused to go; said that what happened with the queen was just some dream I had… I think she blocked the memory out because she’s too afraid to face the truth.”

Kara’s eyebrows furrowed as she frowned, looking down at her desk. Her mom still trusted the queen? Still believed she was a kind and just ruler? What if Victoria had put some sort of spell on her? What if she had a spell on all her subjects, to keep them under her control? Exactly how powerful was this witch?

“Well then we’ll just have to get her back once this is all over,” Lena’s promise broke Kara from her spiraling thoughts, looking up to see her confident face. She gave Kara a reassuring smile, nodding to her. Kara couldn’t help but smile back, her lover’s confidence relaxing all of her anxieties instantly.

Alex silently watched them before suddenly moving to stand. She stretched her arms above her head, stifling a yawn with one hand. By now it was well past sun down, the windows pitch black behind Kara. “I’ll hold you two to that… But for now I think it’s time for me to find some place to sleep off all the excitement of the day.”

Standing at the mention of Alex needing a bed, Kara offered for her sister to sleep in her bed upstairs. Alex waved her off, saying she’d find a bunk down below. Kara showed her sister to the crew’s deck a few stories down, finding her a cot to sleep on in the corner.

“Did you really mean it when you said you trusted her?” Kara asked as Alex sat on the cot. Her sister looked up to her, smiling slightly. “I do… Mostly because I can see how much you trust her, and care for her. I trust your trust, if that makes sense.”

Smiling wide, Kara bent to give her sister one last hug. “Thank you, Alex… That means the world to me.” Alex hummed softly, patting the younger Danvers back. “I love you, Kara… Sleep well.”

Leaving the crew’s dorms, she trotted back up to the main deck just in time to see Lena leaving her quarters. She frowned in disappointment, remembering that she had her own captain’s duties to do on The Devil’s Heart.

“You’re leaving?” Kara tried not to pout as she met Lena just before the gangplank. Her lover sighed, giving her a sympathetic look. “We’ve got to get to the drop-off point as soon as possible, which means I can’t get distracted by your beautiful face for much longer.”

Lena stepped in close, grabbing Kara’s face with both hands. Instinctively Kara reached forward and rested her hands on Lena’s waste, grinning. Cool thumbs rubbed gently along her cheek bones, that ‘Kara-only’ smile pulling at Lena’s lips. “Why don’t you get some sleep? You look exhausted.”

Honestly Kara felt exhausted; she hadn’t been this stressed in a long time. Having the sudden shock of seeing your sister in such a dangerous situation, then having to explain the past seven months of your life while she stared at you like a you were crazy was incredibly draining.

Melting in her lover’s hands, Kara closed her eyes and heavily sighed through her nose. “Today could have gone so much worse… Thank you for trusting me, Love.” She could feel Lena’s smile widening without even having to open her eyes, her own lips pulling into a small grin.

“Of course, Sweetheart… Write me when you’re awake, okay?” The feeling of Lena kissing the tip of her nose made Kara open her eyes, giving a please hum. “Yeah, okay,” she softly promised before leaning in to briefly kiss her lover goodbye.

“I love you, Kara.”

“I love you, too, my Goddess.”

**********

It took another six days for the fleet to find a perfect place to stop and drop off The Devil’s Heart and her well-known ships. It wasn’t like Lena could just anchor down wherever she wanted; There was trading routes they had to avoid, and they couldn’t be too close or too far from land.

With both Lena’s instinctual knowledge of the sea around them thanks to her curse, and Kara’s knowledge of all the trade routes, they managed to find a safe spot to drop off the five ships.

Lena boarded the purple Demon’s Soul as her next ship, while Kara remained with The Oryx’s Revenge. The merchant ship, Sixth Heaven, was sailed by Beck, Lena’s most trusted second mate. The plan was to sail the three ships past the blockade that was positioned around the southern border of Victoria’s kingdom, then sneak onto land.

Alex, who refused to return to the main land, was arranged by Lena to stay on Blue Jay while they went on their mission. She’d be treated as a guest while she was there, and once Kara and Lena returned from their adventure in less than two weeks’ time, they’d sail her back to the island to live in peace.

Before the trio of ships left, however, Kara boarded Blue Jay to say her temporary goodbye’s to her sister. She gave Alex a bone crushing hug, holding her for much longer than normal. “I feel like I’m losing you all over again,” Kara mumbled into her sister’s shoulder, emotion dripping from her voice.

“You’re not,” Alex soothed, “You’ll be back soon… I’ll pray to whoever’s listening every night and every morning that you return safe and sound. We’ll see each other again, Kara.” She squeezed her sister, not pulling away until Kara did.

“I promise, once this is all over, we’ll get mom and be a family again.” She stood up straight, taking a deep breath to sooth her nerves. Alex smiled up at her, nodding in agreement. “Stay safe, Kara… I love you.”

“Love you more.”

With that, Kara turned and walked back onto The Oryx’s Revenge. She watched her crew pull the plank off and untie the ships, feeling Lena surge their ships forward to meet up with the other. Kara waved to Alex as they drifted away, only returning to the helm once she couldn’t see her sister anymore.

Trying to ignore the gnawing worry in her stomach, Kara looked out ahead towards the horizon; towards Lena’s destiny… Her destiny.

It took another two days of sailing before the trio finally reached the blockade. At first it didn’t look like much; just a few ships anchored multiple miles apart, but as Kara got closer she could see the Queen’s naval flag flying high on every single ship.

The Oryx’s Revenge sailed to the port of Sixth Heaven, while Demon’s Soul sailed to the starboard side. They were in perfect formation, with their flags hidden below deck. Kara glanced out to the small group, anxiously gnawing her lip as they aimed to sail between two naval ships.

The entire crew of all three ships seemed to go silent as they all anxiously watched the blockade getting closer and closer, until finally they were just passing through it.

Kara carefully watched the naval ships on either side of them, trying to see if they were drawing up their anchor, or unfurling their sails. It felt like they were staring back, watching them pass through the entire time.

Thankfully, none of the naval ships made a move to follow or stop them. Kara relaxed after they managed to get a few miles away, her shoulders slumping when she realized they were safe.

Giving the command to drop the full sail, Kara wiped her sweaty palms on her pants, not realizing she had been clenching the wheel the entire time. She glanced over to Demon’s Soul, noticing she was also opening her sails completely. She felt them picking up speed, Lena obviously trying to get as far away as possible.

“That was lucky…” Kara mumbled to herself. She glanced behind them for the tenth time, finally allowing herself to completely relax when she realized they were well away from the blockade by now.

That was the easy part; next they had to find a way to get onto land…

Or so Kara thought…

About five hours after passing the blockade, a falcon from Demon’s Soul flew over to Kara. It was Lena’s handwriting, although it seemed to be written out quickly, her typically neat lettering scratchy and sprawled across the scroll.

”To our starboard. Look at the center flag. –Athena”

Frowning at the message, Kara looked past the other ships, in the direction Lena instructed. She noticed five large shapes maybe fifteen miles away, realizing there were ships sailing in their direction.

Pulling her spyglass, Kara felt the same anxiety she felt when she left her sister; like her gut knew something was going to go wrong.

And damn it couldn’t have gone more wrong than this…

Looking at the center ship’s flag, Kara felt her stomach drop.

A golden crown backed by a bright silver shield… Victoria’s flag…

Pulling the spyglass from her eye, Kara stared at the five ships as they sailed directly towards them. There was no way Victoria hadn’t seen the trio, clearly going in their direction.

How had she found them? Was she even on the ship- or was it just her flag, and not her? Could Lena buy them some time and push the fleet away? How were they going to fight off five ships?!

Memories of the fear and panic Kara felt when she’d first seen The Devil’s Heart and her fleet flooded through her. Kara’s face went pale, her palms clammy as she collapsed her telescope and quickly wrote something back to Lena.

”Do you have a plan? –Kara”

She sent the falcon back, pacing as she waited for a reply. It came a few minutes later, but was incredibly vague, Lena clearly busy commanding her ship.

”Go for the center ship. We’ll board ASAP. Stay safe, Sweetheart. –Athena”

Right as Kara finished reading the message, she felt the trio pick up incredible speed, Lena taking them forward into battle. Cursing at the lack of preparation under her breath, Kara quickly commanded for the crew to get ready. She pocketed the note and jumped back to the helm with a shaky breath.

The crew scrambled around the deck, the gunners jumping onto the cannons while their powder monkey’s loaded them up. Every single cannon rolled out into position- about twenty guns on either side of the ship, plus a two four-barreled cannons on the front of the ship.

Kara pulled slightly away from the trio, allowing for some maneuvering room once they got into the thick of it all. Sixth Heaven, which was given some extra artillery from Lena’s other ships, drew back to allow the more armed ships to take the lead.

With the center ship out of the way, Kara was able to turn and see Lena manning the helm of Demon’s Soul, commanding orders. Even from the far distance they were, she could see the determined look on her lovers face.

Swallowing thickly, Kara looked back ahead, noticing that the five ships were getting into their own battle positions. They were getting close incredibly fast, clearing about five miles within a matter of thirty minutes.

It wasn’t until they were about three miles away that suddenly they decreased speed considerably.

Kara furrowed her eyebrows, looking over to see that Demon’s Soul and Sixth Heaven were also slowed to a more natural pace. She looked across the water to see that Lena had stepped away from the helm, leaning over the edge to look down into the water.

It was then that Kara realized that her powers weren’t working.

Now she was certain Victoria was on the fleet of ships- there was no way Lena would suddenly stop them… Which meant that now they were really going to have to fight her hand-to-hand…

Despite the fear that settled low in Kara’s stomach, she could feel something else bubbling up into her chest.

Anger.

Anger over the fact that Victoria was such a coward that she wasn’t willing to fight them on an even level; she’d rather try and pull cheap magic tricks to cripple them, rather than face her own creation.

It made Kara all the more determined to find that bitch and kill her with her own sword.

Gritting her teeth, Kara shifted her feet to stand wide, squaring her shoulders and straightening her back. She glared at the fleet, power pouring off of her in waves as she promised to all the gods paying attention that she’d make sure Victoria paid for everything she did.

“Front cannons; Ready!” Kara called over the crew, her eyes locked onto the battle ahead. Her command was called throughout the deck, reaching down to where the two cannons were stationed.

Two miles left.

“Second from port! Mast!” Kara ordered, figuring they should at least cripple the supporting ships. Next to her, Kara could hear Lena’s authoritative voice yelling at her own crew to prepare to fire.

One mile left.

They needed to be a few hundred feet closer… Closer…

There.

“FIRE!” Kara screamed.

Eight shots billowed from The Oryx’s Revenge. Four shots from Demon’s Soul. Six small shots from Sixth Heaven.

The ship right next to Victoria’s- to Kara’s left –lurched, wood noticeably splintering along its bow. All three of its mast’s fell to the side, crippling the vessel straight away. Lena’s aim hit Victoria’s ship along the bow as well, but the damage was too high for any water to enter the vessel. Sixth Heaven’s small cannon shots missed mostly, but a few hit the far right ship’s hull.

Victoria’s fleet also fired, but most of the ship’s weren’t armed with forward facing guns. “Reload! Fire again!” Kara called, a brief wave of triumph passing over her, seeing that all her shots hit.

It took about a minute before eight more shots rang out, smashing into the same ship she’d hit previously. The bow was now a gaping hole, the vessel noticeably starting to dip forward into the churning waves.

The Oryx’s Revenge echoed with its crew’s cheers, seeing the ship sinking. Smiling to herself, Kara grabbed her wheel and sharply turned her ship to cut left. The ship glided elegantly through the water as her starboard side faced the one remaining ship to her left.

With twenty guns all facing one ship, it was a shocking to not see it go down straight away. Kara had called for all the guns to fire at the one ship, but when the smoke cleared she noticed that less than half her shots hit.

It took Sixth Heaven firing at the ship to the right of Victoria for Kara to realize what had happened.

The cannon balls were being deflected.

As if hitting an invisible barrier, the shots rolled right off and fell into the ocean, like water off of a ducks back.

“Fucking seriously?!” Kara cussed, groaning when she realized that Victoria was using some sort of protection magic. Gritting her teeth, she turned her ship to circle wide around the entire group; maybe she could hit from the side.

Demon’s Soul continued directly straight, firing their own attempt again, only for the shots to miss just like Kara’s.

While Lena aimed to go directly for Victoria, Kara managed to position herself right next to the last remaining ship on the left- of course that meant that they were also open to retaliate.

It was a race to see who could shoot first.

Thankfully The Oryx’s Revenge was much bigger than the relatively small ship, so it was able to sustain the damages it took when the smaller vessel fired first.

Twelve shots were fired across the waters, almost all of them hitting. They scraped through the upper decks, taking out a few crew members, but the cannons and masts were safe. Kara couldn’t see the damage to the hull, but seeing as she wasn’t noticeably taking on water, she didn’t move to check.

“My turn, bitches,” Kara growled, narrowing her eyes at the smaller vessel.

Either Victoria’s magic didn’t protect the sides of the ship, or she wasn’t paying attention to keep the spell up, because all twenty shots hit the naval vessel, decimating it within seconds.

The ship split in half, the two top decks becoming drift wood. Practically nothing was left, water pouring over the wreckage instantly. Kara watched with a smile, quickly realizing that even without Lena’s powers to call upon them, the sea monsters still helped fight her battles; multiple snake and fish like creatures surfaced to devour the forsaken souls down in the water below, their screams swallowed by the mouthful.

The sound of Victoria’s ship firing at her drew Kara’s attention to the similarly sized vessel. She looked up, noticing that Lena had managed to successfully board, it seemed. The two ships were lined together, and even from the distance Kara was, she could see the chaos on the main deck.

Sixth Heaven was somehow keeping up with the battle, working on fighting the two ships that were left while Kara circled her ship around to pull next to Victoria’s ship from behind.
