Stepping away from the helm, Kara commanded Talia to only let her and twenty crew board Victoria’s ship before pulling off to help Sixth Heaven. She trusted Talia enough not to mess up her ship, knowing she couldn’t possible battle Victoria and protect Sixth Heaven at the same time.

With her thorned sword at her side, Kara bolted down to the main deck just as the two ships aligned. They were close enough that Kara didn’t even bother ordering for a plank to be put up. With a running start she managed to use some supplies crates to run up and jump across the gap.

Kara barreled into a man in a navy uniform when she landed, knocking him to the ground. She heard some of her men closely following behind, but for the most part she focused on drawing her sword to block a running slash from another naval soldier.

She let their blades slide against each other, pushing him back with both hands. Kara didn’t even have to deal the killing blow, one of her cannons suddenly going off and taking the man and multiple other soldiers out in one go as it swept across the deck.

Turning back, Kara noticed the crew member who’d fired the shot giving her a thumbs up as The Oryx’s Revenge drifted away to go aid Sixth Heaven. Kara could only briefly scoff at the exchange before turning back to slash at some random soldier’s back.

The battle was between Victoria’s soldiers, and Lena’s crew. Kara’s back up seemed to help greatly, her fighters clearly blood thirsty and battle hungry as they charged into the fray.

Through all the bodies Kara wasn’t able to see where Lena could possibly be. It was hard to look when she was fighting a rather large man who was trying to shoot her with a flint pistol. He aimed at her head, Kara smacking the barrel away with her sword.

Before she could step closer to swing again, he brought the pistol back up, only for Kara to slash at it again. Both fighters gritted their teeth as Kara forced him to back up with every step she took forward.

Again she blocked another attempt to aim at her, but this time she lunged like a fencer. The tip of her flat blade plunged into the unsuspecting man’s chest, burying about two inches into his chest wall. She knew she pierced his lung, feeling the blood splatter across her own chest when he coughed.

Instead of pulling the saber straight out, Kara pushed the blade down and to the side, carving through his lung and ribs like a turkey. The man screamed in agony, falling to the ground. With no time to finish the job, Kara left him there to be trampled, grimacing in regret; as much as she hated these men, it wasn’t her goal to give them such a gruesome death.

Breathing heavily through her nose at the exhaustion of the battle, Kara moved further onto the deck. She slashed and attacked anyone wearing the navy blue or gray uniforms, only stopping to properly fight a few individuals.

Multiple of the soldiers had pistols on them, so at one point Kara stooped to grab one off of a body, firing it point-blank into the head of a large soldier that was beating one of her own fighters with what looked to be an ore.

She helped her crew member up, briefly making sure she was okay before pushing on. Kara was just about to reach the center of the main deck when the sound of a war cry made her turn to look at the source.

A shirtless, bloody soldier with an obnoxiously thick mustache sprinted at her, his sword held low and to the side as he swung to pierce Kara’s right ribs.

She just managed to block the attack, parrying it off to her right. The man ran into her, making her stumble back a few steps. Instinctively Kara slashed up, cutting through the man’s chest and carotid artery as he pressed into her blade, which had been blocking most of his weight from pressing into her.

The man fell as Kara kept moving backwards, freezing when she felt her back hit the back of another figure.

Spinning, Kara’s blade clashed with another blood covered sword, but when her harsh gaze met the familiar sight of Lena, she instantly dropped her stance.

The battle raged on around them, but both women didn’t seem to care. Lena grabbed at the back of Kara’s neck, pulling their foreheads together in an intimate embrace. “God, I was worried I’d never find you… Azazel died before I could even find Victoria, the useless bastard,” Lena’s voice called over the battle around them.

“I’m here, baby- are you okay?” Although there was blood splattered all over her, Lena didn’t seem harmed. She was panting just like Kara, her eyes wide as adrenaline pumped through her. “I’m not hurt, but… Kara, my powers… They’re not working!”

“I know,” Kara soothed, reaching to grab her lover’s waist, “that just means you’ll have to show me those awesome sword fighting skills you boast about all the time.” Lena smiled at the testing joke, the anxiety on her face visibly starting to melt away.

“Are you certain you still want to do this?” Green eyes studied blue, but before Kara could answer, movement out of the corner of her eye made her suddenly pull away and raise her sword arm.

The soldier’s saber clashing against Kara’s was a harsh reminder of where they were as she blocked the arching blow. Lena’s eyes grew cold and cruel as she also turned towards the soldier, who’s attack left his torso defenseless.

Effortlessly Lena drove her sword into the man’s chest, piercing his heart. She drew her blade out with a flourish, spinning on the spot when another soldier charged them. She parried the blow, and Kara came up right behind her to slash down and to the left, cutting straight through the man’s arm.

The two lover’s worked like a well-oiled machine, keeping an eye on the other as they battled together. Lena would block, and Kara would strike- then vice versa. No soldier could get close as they twisted and turned together, moving as one.

Kara just slashed across a soldier’s chest when she noticed Lena had finally looked away, seemingly distracted. She followed her girlfriend’s gaze, spotting who she was looking at instantly.

Victoria…

She was shorter than Kara remembered, having met her when Victoria had given her the task of killing Lena. her hair was a reddish-brown, eyebrows thick and face square. She wore her hair up in a ponytail as she fought off the pirates around her, completely void of any blood.

On her head was a crown that sat just above her brow ridge, curling around her eyes to dip just under her cheekbones and down the bridge of her nose. It rose in multiple spikes, as if molten gold had dripped up against gravity, connecting here in there between spires.

She wore an armored dress of sorts, with a gold and silver chest plate, thin shoulder pieces, and a chainmail skirt that stopped about mid-thigh. Her legs were clad in armor as well, boots unconventionally healed and covered in silver plating.

Victoria didn’t use a physical weapon like a sword or a gun. Instead, she twirled her heavily ringed hands around and visibly called out spells in a foreign tongue before flourishing blasts of silvery-purple magic at anyone close enough.

As if she could feel her arch nemesis looking at her, Victoria turned her head and stared back. Her eyes were a crimson red, little sparks of electricity flickering through the iris as she wickedly smiled.

“Athena!... How good to see you!” Victoria fully turned towards the duo glaring at her, flicking the last bit of purple energy off her hands, hitting one of Kara’s crew effortlessly.

“Victoria,” Lena growled, her eyes ice green as she literally seethed hatred. Kara’s grip tightened on her sword, her stomach churning with fear as they finally faced who they came all this way for.

The false queen only chuckled at the hatred, visibly amused. “I see Azazel failed at his job… Never hire a man to do a woman’s job, I guess.”

“How’d you find us?” Lena questioned, ignoring the comment as she kept her feet firmly planted. Victoria’s smile widened, a cruel sort of laugh erupting from her throat. “You’d be surprised how far a tracking spell can reach… All I had to do was wait for you to get close enough. I’ve been expecting you ever since you went up to get that little merchant ship of yours.”

Victoria tilted her head towards the battle that raged on over the water, but her red eyes didn’t leave Lena. So she knew they were coming days before they even reached the blockade… Which made Kara question exactly what else Victoria could do.

“I see you’ve finally grown the balls to come after me… What made you change your mind?” Victoria took a few more steps closer, that cruel smile still on her thin lips.

“I did,” Kara spoke up, still tightly holding her sword as she anxiously glanced between Victoria and her hands.

As if noticing Kara for the first time, her red eyes narrowed as they looked over towards the blonde captain. “Ah… So the dead captain and the villain-turned-hero come together to try and kill off their common enemy… I must say, I’m very disappointed in your ability to follow orders, Kara Danvers.”

Clenching her jaw, Kara glared at the insult. “I’m not dead, bitch,” she snarled, trying her hardest to wait and see when Lena would attack with her.

The rude name made Victoria narrow her eyes, the smirk dropping from her face. “No, you’re not dead,” she mumbled, “… But you will be.”

Suddenly a jumble of words poured from her mouth, a practiced tongue saying the spell in half a second. Crackling purple energy swelled around Victoria’s hand as she sharply inhaled, throwing it in a ball at the duo.

Lena had just enough time to react, shoving Kara to the side as she jumped the other direction. They both landed heavily onto the bloody deck, Kara grunting at the heavy fall. She didn’t have time to revel in the uncomfortable pain, already scrambling up to run at Victoria.

She called another spell, throwing a second ball of energy at Kara, who managed to dodge to the left just in time. Lena was also up, trying to get closer, but Victoria said another spell that made one of the bodies they were stepping over twitch to life.

Its hand lurched forward, grabbing onto Lena’s ankle. She fell to the ground with a gasp, but Kara was too busy dodging another ball of energy to get anywhere close to help her.

With Lena tangled up with the undead creature, Victoria gave her full effort to Kara. Ball after ball was thrown at her, who ducked and weaved, diving on the deck as she struggled not to get hit. Something told her that she wouldn’t be getting back up if one of them did manage to touch her.

Gritting her teeth, Kara jumped back up after stumbling backwards, right as Victoria threw another ball of energy. At that moment the moving bodies of the battle around crowed Kara, so she couldn’t just fall to the side again.

Gasping in a mixture of shock and fear, realizing she was about to get hit, Kara squeezed her eyes closed and brought her sword- still in her hand –up in a diagonal block instinctively. She braced, prepared to get hit directly in the chest.

But no blast of energy came.

Instead, she felt something impact the blade of her sword, her shoulder flinching from the recoil. Opening her eyes, Kara realized that she managed to block the crackling energy, sending the ball back towards Victoria.

She let out a yelp as she ducked away from the returned fire, almost stumbling onto her knees. She looked up to Kara, giving a frustrated growl.

Victoria was just about to stand up when suddenly Lena’s boot came out of the crowed and smashed against her chest plate. She fell backwards onto her butt as Lena fully appeared, her sword arching high to bring it down on her nemesis.

But suddenly an invisible force blasted against Lena’s chest in a wave, sending her flying back. Kara gasped, watching her land a few yard behind her in a crumpled ball.

She was just about to turn and run back to make sure she was okay, but that familiar crackling noise made her stop.

Realizing that Victoria was about to throw another ball of energy at her, Kara again blocked the blow. She slashed at it to give it some more power as it shot back at Victoria, who flourished her hand and pushed to the side with the force spell.

Kara’s accidental distraction gave Lena just enough time to find a gun and shoot it from behind her lover. The bullet flew just under Kara’s arm, and pierced straight through the lower right side of Victoria’s torso when she was busy deflecting her own spell, an involuntary gasp pulled from her lungs.

She flinched at the impact, red eyes going wide as a ringed hand went to press against the pierced armor. Blood pooled through her fingers, pain briefly registering across Victoria’s face before being replaced with rage.

With a frustrated yell, she mumbled a spell, tossing her free hand carelessly back towards the helm behind her. In a swirl of purple smoke, she disappeared, before shortly reappearing just behind the wheel of the ship.

Kara started, turning to look back at her lover, to see if she was still behind her, but Lena was already sprinting towards the helm. Kara surged to follow a few yards behind, surprised by Lena’s speed. The battle managed part perfectly for the two women, allowing Lena to thunder up the stairs to where Victoria had fled.

Kara was shortly behind her, sword still in hand just as she heard Victoria growl out another spell. It hit Lena full-force; a concussive blast that sent her flying backwards, pinning her to the floor- like an invisible bolder was pressing against her chest.

Kara winced at the blast, expecting to also be hit.

But instead of getting blown back, she was pulled forward.

Kara’s boots slid against the deck as she tried to stop the invisible hand that pulled her into Victoria, lurching to a stop with a painful pressure against her windpipe.

The shorter woman grasped her throat with an iron grip, teeth gritting as she glared up at Kara. Her free hand grabbed Kara’s sword arm, sliding towards her wrist to painfully twist it until she dropped her thorned saber.

Victoria’s red eyes were glowing, silver electricity flaring through the color menacingly as she sneered at Kara. Angrily, her heeled boot cracked painfully into Kara’s knee, kicking her leg out from under her.

Falling to a kneeling position, Kara choked against the hand holding her throat, trying to fight the other hand gripping her arm. She couldn’t see- couldn’t hear –Lena behind her. All she saw was the rage in Victoria’s eyes.

“I told you; you’re going to die…”

Suddenly Victoria’s grip on Kara’s arm dropped. She stooped and picked up the thorned sword from the deck of the ship, briefly admiring the craftsmanship.

Kara’s eyes widened, realizing what was about to happen. She gripped Victoria’s hand on her throat with both hands, willing her to stop.

Somewhere off in the distance Lena was screaming, still trapped under the restriction spell; her word were unintelligible over the thundering heartbeat in Kara’s ears.

Victoria gripped the sword, blade pointing down as she brought it right over Kara’s heart.

“Any last words?”

Kara’s breath came out in quick pants as a million things ran through her head. Part of her wanted to be confident and promise Victoria’s death, but in that moment she realized she’d made a mistake…

…It wasn’t her destiny to kill Victoria.

It was Lena’s…

Suddenly her mind filled with thoughts of her lover, who was still pinned to the deck somewhere behind her.

She thought of when they first met; how they hated one another… How they wasted so many months avoiding their true feelings, too busy thinking they should be enemies.

That was probably the only regret that Kara felt as she thought about the woman she loved with all her heart; how they didn’t have enough time together…

“I-… Athena- Lena!... I love you!”

The feeling of her own sword driving through her heart was almost as painful as hearing her lover’s blood chilling scream a few feet away.

Chapter End Notes

I promised myself to never kill off a main character- and I will still live by that promise... You guys are going to have to trust me on this one.
I'll update ASAP, but I've got a ton of projects due in the next few days, so it might be a while.

Everything will be okay,
-Aries

Broken Curse

Chapter Summary

It's over... It's finally over...

Chapter Notes

Yeah, no, I'm too impatient to make you guys wait for this next chapter.
I hope this is a satisfying ending to the final battle, and Victoria!

This has been an amazing journey, thank you all!!!
-Aries

See the end of the chapter for more notes

How…

How did this go so wrong?!

Kara and Lena are fighting side by side as if they were one being… But the next thing Lena knew, she was pinned to the ground by some invisible force, watching the love of her life get pierced through her heart.

The weight on Lena’s chest was excruciating, pressing into her ribs and sternum so hard it felt like her bones were going to break. She thrashed her legs against the deck, trying to find traction to push herself up against the force.

She was begging and pleading for Kara to get up- to do something -but when she heard her final words and saw the blade of her gifted sword drive into her heart, piercing through her back, all Lena could do was scream.

Her throat hurt, her chest hurt, her heart hurt.

Everything hurt…

Uselessly Lena tried to reach out and grasp at Kara as she visibly gasped at the pain, tensing as she held onto Victoria’s wrist. She was only a few feet away, why couldn’t she reach her?!

Tearing her eyes away from Kara, Lena’s eye’s bore into Victoria’s face.

That BITCH… She was smiling!

Red eyes turned to look at Lena as she screamed at the top of her lungs, tears burning across her temple and the bridge of her nose as she watched her worst nightmare happen right in front of her.

Kara’s shoulders started to visibly weaken, her grip slipping from Victoria’s wrist to hang loosely at her sides as blood pooled across her clothing, dripping from the tip of her own sword.

“No! No, no, NO! KARA!”

The world went completely silent, everything around her going into slow motion. Lena’s vision turned black all around her and Kara as she stared down at her lifeless face, eyes wide in realization.

She was trying to speak- to say a spell or fight against the bonds holding her –but Lena made sure to keep her mouth shut.

The armor started to slip off into the puddle of bloody goo as Victoria’s vocal cords deteriorated to the point where she could no longer scream. Her blood sizzled audibly as it hit the wooden deck below until there was nothing left.

The last thing to fall was her cracking crown, shattering across the deck into a million pieces.

“Don’t worry… She’ll be in so much more pain where she’s going…”

A wave of realization that the long awaited battle was finally over poured over Lena as she tore her eyes away from Victoria’s melted body, noticing that the navy around them had been defeated.

She didn’t even think twice, stepping to press into her lover’s broad frame as she grabbed two fist-fulls of her bloody coat. It felt so amazing being able to feel Kara’s lips moving against her own once again, Lena’s heart thundering with excitement.

There was cheering around them from both of their crews, everyone celebrating the hard fought battle. Lena refused to pull away from the kiss, but at the sound of the cries of victory Kara leaned slightly away. She was breathing heavily, her forehead pressed to her lover’s as she kept her eyes squeezed closed.

“How?... How am I still here?” Kara questioned, her voice soft against the loud cheers. “Victoria had her magic, and I had mine,” Lena answered simply, too distracted by her desire to keep kissing Kara to fully answer the question.

She felt Kara smile into the kiss, her strong fingers tangling with Lena’s dark hair. She couldn’t help but smile as well, placing a few more kisses against her lips before pulling away to look at her face. “I love you- I love you so much,” Lena babbled, committing her lover’s face to memory, “I seriously thought I lost you- god I love you so much!”

Kara gave a relieved laugh, her fingers tightening in Lena’s hair before carefully pulling out to touch her heated cheeks. “You’re so warm,” she noticed, blue eyes dazzling with excitement. Lena smiled, pressing her still bloody hands against the back of Kara’s.

“You broke my curse, Sweetheart… Our blood, together- your sacrifice… I’m free!”

“How do you feel?” Kara searched Lena’s face with shocked but curious blue eyes, her smile one of the best things Lena had ever seen in a long time.

She paused for a moment, taking a quick inventory of what was going on inside of herself. “I feel… Exhausted…” Her muscles ached, along with her chest and hands. It felt like she’d never slept a day in her life, her mind and body sluggish.

The musical laugh that emanated from Kara sent a wave of warmth through Lena as she listened to the beautiful sound. “I don’t doubt that… Let’s get everyone out of here; I’m sure we all want to go home.” Lena was nodding before Kara was even finished talking, the idea of finally leaving this god-forsaken ship giving her a small boost of energy.

The entire deck was littered with bodies- pirate and navy alike –as Lena led the way down from the helm. She frowned at her crew members lost lives, but was satisfied to see that most of the carnage was that of Victoria’s men.

Lena’s crew took care of their own, taking any wounded or dead over to Demon’s Soul while she used her powers to guide The Oryx’s Revenge and Sixth Heaven from where they were a few miles away. They were both surprisingly still floating, although Sixth Heaven was visibly crippled with the amount of damage it had taken.

Crossing over to Demon’s Soul, Kara helped toss any dead navy men off the side of the ship, to feed the hungry sea monsters that had still come to Lena’s aid, despite her suppressed abilities. It was amusing how much energy she had, despite the hard fought battle, and the fact that she had been stabbed through the chest only an hour ago.

Once the ship was cleared off and untied from the floating coffin that had once been Victoria’s ship, Lena used the currents to push them away from one another. Her face hardened as she looked at the ship of her enemy, making a last second decision to make certain Victoria would never come back.

“Starboard guns, get ready!” Her voice dripped with authority as she trotted up to the helm. Her crew scrambled to man the cannons, the powder monkeys loading them up as fast as possible. As soon as Victoria’s ship was about a hundred yards away, Lena gave the order to fire.

Countless shots went off not only from Demon’s Soul, but also from The Oryx’s Revenge as she rounded around the back of Victoria’s ship. Kara, who remained on the main deck, glanced to her ship with a proud smile, cheering with the crew.

Lena watched as the massive ship was decimated in only a few seconds. The entire bottom of the hull was shot out from under itself, the top slowly tilting to the side before landing in the water with a massive splash.

No one left the side of the ship until they were all certain Victoria’s vessel was deep in the depths of the ocean. Lena glared at it for a long moment before stepping away to grab the wheel of her ship. “Full sails!” She ordered, watching everyone run to their stations to fulfill her order.

Kara trotted up the stairs to where Lena steered, grinning to her lover. “Can you bring me back to my ship?” She questioned, gesturing to where The Oryx’s Revenge was just about to unfurl her sails, having picked up the remaining crew on Sixth Heaven.

“Only if you let me board with you,” Lena replied with a smirk, happiness swelling in her chest at the sound of her lover’s laugh. “Of course! You’ve got over seven years of sleep to catch up on.”

**********

Lena flounced down onto the bed, groaning softly as her face buried into the pillows. The fresh clothing she had changed into only a few minutes ago felt so comfortable compared to the blood and sweat covered outfit Lena had been wearing earlier.

Having already kicked off her boots, Lena curled further into Kara’s bed as she rolled onto her side, watching her lover pull her bloody jacket from her shoulders. Even though Kara’s head was tilted down, Lena could see that she was frowning as she looked at herself.

“Are you okay?” Without thinking she stood, ignoring her complaining muscles as she moved to where her lover stood. “Yeah, I just… I’m processing how close we were to loosing each other…”

Lena frowned, green eyes growing sad as she remembered the fear she had felt when Kara died earlier that day. “Yeah, well… We’re still here, and that’s all that matters,” she soothed, moving into her lover’s space.

“But-“ Kara started, her eyes growing misty. Lena interrupted her before she could spiral into those unhappy thoughts, refusing to let their victory be muddled with ‘what could have been’.

“Baby, I’ve spent so many years of my life focused on what happened to me and my loved ones so long ago… We can’t look on the past with regret. What matters now is that you’re still here with me, and she’s gone.”

The deep breath that Kara took in was shaky as she struggled to look away from her bloody, torn shirt. She reached up and pressed her warm palms against Lena’s equally warm cheeks. Smiling at the sensation, Kara leaned in and gently kissed her lover.

“I’m so happy you’re still here,” Lena whispered into their shared space. She looked at the hole in Kara’s shirt, trying to push the last little bit of lingering fear from her mind. “Let me help you clean up, okay?”

Wordlessly Kara nodded, smiling sweetly at the offer. Leaning back slightly to see what she was doing, Lena untucked Kara’s ruined shirt from her pants. She softly instructed her lover to lift her arms, carefully pulling the fabric over her head.

There was a bright red scar that ran about three inches down Kara’s sternum, freshly healed after the exchanging of soul’s that Death permitted. Lena ghosted her fingers over the scar, softly chuckling as she whispered, “We’re matching.”

Kara smiled at the comparison, watching Lena step away to grab a wash cloth. She dipped it in the basin of water on the vanity, ringing it out before moving over to wash the dried blood off her chest. Kara reached around her back and silently undid her bra, easily slipping it off of her shoulders and tossing it in the pile of ruined clothing.

Lena made sure to wash every spot and smear of blood off of Kara’s chest, dipping low to leave a trail of kisses along her collar bone and shoulders. “You’re so beautiful,” she whispered between kisses, feeling Kara smiling against the top of her head, the hands on her hips tightening.

Once all the blood was washed off, Lena discarded the cloth with the clothing, helping Kara remove her pants and underwear. She moved way to grab some comfortable clothing, while her lover put on a fresh set of undergarments now that she was clean, both of them giving of an air of happiness.

With both women dressed and ready to finally sleep away the soreness in their muscles, Lena took Kara’s hand and walked backwards, guiding her to bed. They softly kissed here and there, but as soon as Kara was wrapped tightly in Lena’s arms, the affection was replaced with the need for sleep.

It was odd for Lena to be able to close her eyes and feel the grips of unconsciousness pulling at the corners of her mind. But with Kara right next to her, it wasn’t nearly as scary or foreign as she thought it would be.

With a deep sigh, the last thing Lena registered before finally falling asleep for the first time in over seven years was;

“I’m so glad it’s over… Finally over…”

A Life Worth Living

A collection of fluff scenes as a bonus chapter.

Chapter Notes

It’s an incredibly weird feeling to be hungry after seven years of not eating.

Or so Lena says, after she groggily woke up that morning.

Her hair was an uncharacteristic mess as she sat up and looked around Kara’s upper quarters. The make-up she didn’t even think to remove was a mess under her eyes, making her sleepy squint even more noticeable.

Kara had already woken up a few hours ago, and was just sitting against the head board of the bed as she read a book. When Lena had suddenly sat up with a sharp inhale, looking around the room like it did something insulting and mumbled, “Food,” in a scratchy voice Kara had to cover her mouth not to laugh.

“Are you hungry, baby girl?” Kara asked, kicking her blankets off to go get something for Lena to nibble on. She figured it’d be a bad idea to introduce a full meal to Lena straight away; she might get sick, and that wouldn’t be a great first impression to mortality again.

Lena just grunted at Kara’s question, watching her walk around the bed. She quickly noted that her lover definitely wasn’t a morning person, and that she would probably have to steer clear of her for another ten minutes while her mind got itself up and running.

Grabbing some fruit and a flask of water, Kara paused to make sure Talia- her new first mate; promoted after the fantastic fighting she did the day before –was good to keep sailing in her absence. “I’m more than happy to take care of her, Captain; enjoy your day off, you’ve earned it.”

Talia’s hazel eyes glanced down at Kara’s chest, probably having heard what happened on Victoria’s ship via the grape vine. Kara was wearing one of her white flowing shirts, the front tie open just enough so that you could see the top of her scar, the shirt tucked into some high-wasted riding pants.

“Thank you- Captain Athena’s got some things she’s having to get used to all at once,” Kara explained with a grateful smile to her new first mate. Talia hummed in amused understanding, letting her captain trot off to go give her grumpy girlfriend some food.

Lena was out of bed when Kara made it back up to her bedroom. She was washing her face, her hair slightly wet and slicked back out of her face. She seemed to have woken up enough to notice that she looked like an adorable mess.

When Kara returned with the food she requested, Lena smiled lovingly. “You look beautiful… Almost as beautiful as those grapes,” she joked, gesturing to the plate. Her voice was still sleepy, but at least she could now make full sentences.

Kara laughed at the comment, setting the food and drink on the side table next to her bed. She went over to Lena, leaning down to kiss her lover’s cheek before suddenly wrapping her arms under her butt, lifting her off the ground.

Lena squealed, grasping Kara’s shoulders as they walked back to the bed. “Come on, let’s eat something,” Kara laughed as she gently flopped her girlfriend down onto the bed. Lena huffed her freshly-fixed hair out of her face, looking up at Kara as she stood beside the bed.

“Are you upset that I said a piece of fruit is more beautiful than you?” She joked, sitting up to poke at the blonde captain’s side. Kara laughed again, flinching away from the tickling touch. “No, but if you keep talking about a grape like that, maybe I will!”

Grabbing one of the mentioned grapes off of the vine, Kara held it for Lena to eat from her fingers, both of them laughing and smiling together. “Don’t worry, Sweetheart,” Lena soothed as she leaned forward to take the grape, “You can do so much for me than this food can.”

Gently taking the fruit in her mouth, Lena slightly leaned away, watching her lover turn bright red at the playful yet flirty look she’d given her. She grinned before biting into the firm grape in her mouth, inhaling sharply as sweet tasting juice burst across her tongue.

It’d been a long time since she had such a sensation; she never bothered holding a piece of food in her mouth, considering it rude to just spit someone’s cooking out, so instead she just settled for ‘drinking’. It was a strange feeling to be able to actually bite into something and chew.

Kara watched her with sparkling blue eyes as Lena ate the grape, her eyes fluttering closed as she savored the taste. “Good?” Kara softly questioned as she leaned down and kissed the corner of Lena’s mouth. “So good…”

Smiling wide, she watched her girlfriend swallow the grape before leaning to the side to grab a handful more. “I honestly thought there was something wrong when I woke up and my stomach made a noise,” Lena mumbled through her mouthful of fruit.

“I would to, if I hadn’t been hungry for seven years,” Kara agreed as she toed off her boots. Lena shifted to sit with her back against the headboard, happily munching on her plate of fruit as Kara crawled into bed again.

“How long was I asleep?” Lena quirked an eyebrow, her eyes focusing on the wall of windows against the back wall. She could see that it was bright out by now, and Kara was in a whole other outfit. “A little under eighteen hours?” Kara answered, smiling when Lena’s eyes went wide in shock.

“Eighteen hours?! Are the ships okay!? God, I didn’t mean to sleep that much!” She started to get up and out of bed, but Kara’s strong arm wrapped around her waist and pulled her into the bed again. “I’ve already checked on both ships, and let our crew’s know that we’re taking a day to recoup and get you used to being curse-less.”

The amount of love in Lena’s face as she looked at Kara was indescribable. She dropped the slice of orange she was about to try back onto the plate and moved to straddle Kara’s thighs. “That settles it… You’re my soulmate.”

Kara smiled wide as she instinctively let her hands rest on Lena’s hips. “I’m glad we’re on the same page,” she replied in a soft voice, blue eyes flickering down to full lips. Lena leaned forward and connected their lips softly, sighing at the feeling of having Kara hold her again.

They both relaxed into each other, the kiss soft and slow. Kara’s hands slid up Lena’s back, hugging her tightly. Lena’s fingers played with loose golden curls, smiling so wide she had to break the kiss for a moment.
