Kara stepped off of The Oryx’s Revenge, walking down the steep plank that led down to the sandy beach below. Lena had instructed her to take her into the repair bay on the island to get the damage from the Queen’s Battle- as they were calling it –fixed, as well as Demon’s Soul.

Alex followed closely behind her younger, yet larger sister. Her eyes were wide and observant as she looked around, clearly in awe with everything around her. Kara felt a sense of pride for her new home, excited to see how much it would impress Alex.

“This… Is not what I expected,” her sister admitted as they strolled across the beach to meet Lena in the harbor’s town. “What were you expecting?” Kara asked, looking back towards Alex with a grin. “Something much smaller… And more ‘barbaric’?”

Kara scoffed, unable to be offended, because she too thought an island of pirates was going to be a little more lawless than this. “Well, you’ll be in for lots more surprises if you keep expecting that,” she chuckled, facing forward when she heard the whinny of horses.

Enzo tossed his head as he looked to Kara, having spotted her before she spotted him. Seeing her new beloved horse sent a wave of excitement, yet satisfaction that she was really home, through her. She smiled wide at her stallion, calling for the stable hand holding him to drop the reins.

As soon as Enzo was free, he practically galloped up to Kara, putting his massive head right into her waiting hands. She softly cooed to him, petting his golden coat sweetly. “Who’s this handsome fellow?” Alex asked, moving to pat at Enzo’s shoulder.

Kara explained his story, and how they got to meet as she grabbed her mount’s reins and led them to where a second horse- Alex’s horse –was waiting. They got acquainted quickly, the brown, black, and white paint mare sweetly nuzzling at Alex’s pockets for any snacks to steal from.

Once the Danvers sisters were both mounted and ready to travel, Kara showed Alex down the path that would take them into the small town. Lena was already there, sitting on Outlaw and calling out to someone across the street.

When she spotted Kara riding up, she smiled wide. “Ready to go?” She asked as her lover got closer, glancing to Alex who was still looking around in surprise. “More than ready… I want to go home so bad!” Lena smiled at Kara’s words, humming in agreement.

“Well then let’s go home… Do you need a minute, Alex?” Lena tilted her head as she spoke to the elder Danvers, chuckling at her overwhelmed look. “Uh… No, we can go, I just… This is a lot…” Both Lena and Kara laughed, Lena smiling wide. “This isn’t anything, yet… You’ll be falling out of your saddle once we reach National City.”

Alex’s eyebrows shot up, visibly wondering how she could be any more blown away. “Oh boy,” she mumbled under her breath, exasperated as Lena moved to lead the group through the small village. “I was overwhelmed, too,” Kara admitted as she and Alex followed, “but it gets better after a while.”

They talked about the island and what Alex should expect for a few hours, but as they traveled the conversation smoothly led itself. They took breaks and spent the night- where Lena was actually able to use the cot in her tent –but finally, after three long days of travel they finally got to national city.

Lena had been right; Alex was almost falling out of her saddle as she watched National City appear over the protective hill and mountains that surrounded it. “Holy… Shitballs…” was all she could mumble, eyes wide and jaw in her lap.

Kara watched her with an amused look, turning to Lena to question if that’s how she looked when she first saw National City. “Pretty much,” Lena laughed, “Although you were a lot more reluctant to show how impressed you were.”

Seeing her home, after almost a month of traveling, Kara couldn’t help but pick up her pace a little bit more. “Come on, let’s go see Sam!” She called back to Lena and Alex, smiling wide. Her sister looked confused who ‘Sam’ was, but Lena looked just as excited to see her best friend as Kara.

Pushing a little bit faster, they made their way up to the castle in no time. After taking a moment to unsaddle the horses, they all went into the castle. “You’ve seriously lived in this place for seven months?” Alex asked Kara quietly, staring up at the tall walls just before they entered.

Kara agreed with a beaming smile, happy to see that her sister thought it was just as beautiful as her. As she was talking about all the rooms she wanted to show her sister- her room, the throne room, the balcony that showed all of the city –Lena led the way through the castle.

They headed for the banquet hall, everyone tired and hungry from their long journey. But they didn’t even get that far before Sam’s figure barreled straight into Lena, having come from a side hallway. She bear hugged her best friend, her face buried in Lena’s shoulders as she spoke a million miles an hour.

“Oh my god, I heard everything that happened! Someone wrote a letter when you guys got back- Oh my god, your curse! It’s broken!” She suddenly let go of Lena, and latched onto Kara, the hug bone crushing. “And you! You helped her- wait, are you okay?! I read that you got injured, but Athena saved you!”

Kara couldn’t help but laugh at her friend’s concern, unpinning her arms from her sides to hug Sam back. “We’re all okay, you don’t have to worry,” she soothed, looking past her wild brown hair to smile at Lena, who watched them with an amused look.

“Actually, we even managed to find my sister while we were out,” Kara pointed out, realizing that Alex was staring at the entire interaction with pure confusion on her face. Sam pulled out of the hug, a comically wide smile on her face as she started to turn and extend a hand to Alex.

But just before any greeting could get out of her mouth, she visibly paused, her smile faltering into a look of surprise. “I- Oh… Uh- hi. Hello… Uhm, hi, I’m… What’s your name?” Sam’s cheeks practically glowed with how red they were getting, her eyes wide as she stared at the elder Danvers sister.

Alex also stared for a moment, finally getting to look at Sam’s face. It took her a moment to realize that Sam was even speaking to her, her eyebrows furrowed as she tried to decipher what was just asked of her. “I, uh- I’m sorry?”

Kara raised an eyebrow at the strange interaction, having never seen Sam- or her sister -get so flustered so quickly. “I-I’m Sam,” she stumbled, not answering Alex’s question what so ever. Sam extended her hand properly, Kara’s sister fumbling as she tried to take it in her own.

“Oh, hi… I’m Alex, by the way.” They both held eye contact, slowly shaking the other’s hand for a much longer-than-normal amount of time. Kara glanced between the two women, then over to Lena. She gave her lover a ‘is this really happening?’ look, Lena smirking and nodding.

There was a long pause where no one said anything, Sam and Alex simply shaking one another’s hand. Realizing that they weren’t going to break out of their weird moment together, Kara decided to step in. She cleared her throat, stepping so they could both see her. “Now that introductions are out of the way, how about we go get something to eat?”

Alex quickly dropped her hand, stiffly nodding as she visibly tried to not keep staring at Sam. “Food sounds fantastic,” she mumbled, glancing one more time at this new woman before awkwardly trying to shuffle away.

Sam managed to get herself together just a little bit more than Alex, taking in a deep breath before she started to walk next to Lena. “You didn’t tell me Kara had a hot sister,” Sam whisper-yelled. Kara snorted, glancing to see if Alex had heard the very badly concealed conversation.

Judging by how bright red Alex’s face was, she definitely heard it.

Kara couldn’t help but chuckle, her smile wide at how amusing the situation was; she’d never really known her sister to be interested in- much less get flustered by –another person. She had been single practically all of Kara’s life, and always got awkward at the idea of dating someone.

As the group walked into the banquet, Sam glanced back over towards Alex, having continued to talk in a much more hushed voice to Lena. It was clear she wanted to talk with her a bit more, and even with Alex’s refusal to even look in her general direction, Kara could see that Alex was interested in talking as well.

“How about you show Alex around after dinner, Sam? I’m sure she’d love to see all the hidden nooks and crannies you know,” Kara casually suggested, noticing the sly smirk Lena flashed her.

“I would really love that,” Alex blurted, finally turning to look at Sam, who’s eyebrows shot up almost to her hairline. “Oh, really? Yeah, I could definitely do that!” Sam smiled wide, falling back a few steps so that Alex could meet up with her.

Deciding to give them some room, Kara strode over to where Lena walked in front of them, heading for their head table. “That was hilarious,” Kara snickered, looking over her shoulder to see that her sister and Sam had already stopped to talk- Sam was pointing around the dining hall, already starting their tour.

Lena also watched her best friend and Alex instantly starting to connect, smirking again. “You’re so evil… But yes, it was quite funny.” She turned to Kara, her smirk turning into a smile. “Maybe they’ll actually get together?”

“I give it a month,” Kara chuckled.

**********

The port of Midvale was a very busy part of the town; ships were going in and out constantly, and a large majority of businesses were lined along the water’s edge, so there was a lot of foot traffic.

Kara anxiously nibbled at her lip as she stared at her old home. They’d just managed to find a place to tie The Oryx’s Revenge, and her and Alex were about to go off into the city to see if their mother wasn’t in town still.

The elder Danvers waited patiently for Kara, having promised her before they left that she’d return to the main land, despite her swearing not to three months ago. Even though Victoria was long dead, she still had a distain for the land she once called her home, much like Kara.

It took a few extra minutes, but eventually the blonde captain sighed, adjusted the sword at her side, and walked to meet Alex at the edge of the gangplank. “Ready?” Alex asked, watching her sister approach.

“No, but we can’t wait all day… Are you certain she’s still here? She might have run off, just like you.” Kara tucked her thumbs into her pockets, anxiously looking to her sister. “She seemed pretty content on staying put when I tried to convince her to leave… I’m sure with you showing up, it’ll change her mind.”

Kara sighed, glancing down at her shoes. She was worried that her mother wouldn’t want to come back, and the last thing Kara wanted to do was leave her family behind again. Although Alex’s confidence about Eliza following them once she knew Kara was safe and alive set her at ease- but not by much.

“Okay, if you say so… Let’s go, before anyone starts asking questions.” Using all her willpower, Kara took a step onto the gangplank. And then another. One foot after the other.

As soon as Kara’s boots were on the cobblestone of the pier they’d docked on, she glanced back to her ship. She took in a deep, slow breath before turning and making her way into the crowed with Alex. The streets were busy, Alex grabbing the sleeve of Kara’s jacket as they tried to push through the traffic to get to a less crowded street.

Thankfully both the girls knew the town like the back of their hand, so they managed to find a back alley that took them away from the market area, where everyone liked to flock. “Geeze, that was a lot,” Kara mumbled, glancing behind them with a slight frown.

Alex hummed in agreement, patting her ruffled shirt down, after a few people had bumped rudely into her. “Makes me realize how good we have it back on the island,” she mumbled half to herself, half to Kara.

The comment made the younger Danvers pause, eyebrow’s raising. “So you’re enjoying it there?” There’d always been that vague worry in the back of Kara’s mind that her sister wouldn’t like National City as much as her, even though Alex seemed perfectly happy with where she was now.

“I’m loving it there, Kara! I was a little skeptical at first, but Sam’s been really helpful in explaining everything to me, and Lena’s been fantastic in making it feel like home. I really need to find a way to pay her back for getting me that cottage in the Housing District.” Alex glanced to her sister, a genuine smile pulling at her lips.

Mirroring the smile, Kara felt a little bit of her anxiety leaving; if Alex loved National City, then so would Eliza. They just had to convince her to join them, is all.

Keeping up with the casual conversation as they turned down another street, Kara jumped onto the topic of National City, and Sam. “So you two have been getting along? You’re both really close, it seems.”

Alex had been living in National City for three months now, and even though their lines of work didn’t put them together constantly, Kara noticed that Sam would often go out of her way to go down and find something to do with her sister. It was clear that they were getting closer with each day, and Kara was curious to find out what that meant.

The deep blush on Alex’s face told her that her suspicions about her sister’s budding feelings had been true. “Yeah, we uh… We’re super close… To the point where we’re questioning if we should move further or not.” Alex scratched the back of her neck; a sign that she was really troubled over this.

“What makes you guys think you shouldn’t? It’s clear you two have a thing for one another,” Kara pointed out, slowing their pace so that they had a little more time to talk.

“Well… I guess it’s just me who’s a little reluctant to move forward. Sam’s made it really obvious that she’d like to start dating.” Alex paused, visibly trying to decide if she wanted to add something or not.

“She even kissed me before we left National City.”

Kara’s steps faltered, pausing as she rounded to look at her sister. Her eyes were wide; excitement clear on her face. “Wait, seriously?!” Alex flinched at the sudden movement, shuffling away from Kara’s energy. The light blush on her cheeks deepened, nodding as she pushed to continue walking.

Hurrying to catch up, Kara had a million questions running through her head. “Did you say anything to her?”

“I asked her to give me some time to think. I told her by the end of the trip, I’ll tell her if I’m ready to move forward with the relationship or not.”

“Aleeex! Why didn’t you jump on the chance to go out with her?!”

“Because I’m worried it might end bad!... I don’t want to leap into a relationship too fast, and then it turns out we’re not good together. I’d prefer to have a friendship and repressed feelings, rather than no friendship and a broken heart.”

Kara frowned, understanding her sister’s caution, even if she knew there was no reason for her to be so scared. “I don’t think you guys would end in a burning ball of fire. I saw how you two have been looking at one another since the moment you met; there’s enough of a connection that if or when you do run into some sort of trouble, you’ll both be willing to work it out.”

“How can you be so sure?” Alex frowned, furrowing her eyebrows just like Kara does when she’s uncertain and frustrated. “Because that’s how every true relationship goes; you both know there’s something worth fighting for, so of course you’ll fight for it.”

Alex gave a deep sigh, rubbing the back of her neck again. “I don’t know, Kara…”

“You’ll never get what you want if you don’t take a leap of faith… I had to, when I first got with Lena.”

“What do you mean? You two are literally perfect.”

“You wouldn’t say that if you’d seen us when we first met- but that’s not the point here. The point is, is that I had no idea how we were going to turn out; we were enemies for the longest time, and I wasn’t sure if I could really forgive her for everything she’d caused.”

Kara paused, looking thoughtfully down at her feet as they walked. “But I decided that my feelings for her were stronger than my anger at our past… I had to jump off of that cliff, and dive into the deep end without knowing if Lena even felt the same- for all I knew, she still considered me a servant.”

“I guess that makes sense… I’m just scared of losing someone I really care about in the end.” Kara gave Alex a reassuring smile, reaching over to grab her hand and squeeze. “Your anxiety is valid, but I hope me telling you that I’m certain you two will turn out perfect gives you something to hold on to.”

The two Danvers sisters smiled to one another, Alex noticeably lighter now that she’d said what was on her mind the entire trip.

They walked through the streets that they had once played in as small children, finally coming to a stop at the tall gates of their childhood home.

The Danvers had never been an incredibly rich family by any means, but Alex’s late father’s business as a scientist and inventor certainly gave them enough money to buy a big home with a decent amount of land. The house was on the outside of town, allowing for a long, winding road to lead them through the large yard and up to the house.

Kara stared at the building as they approached, her nerves returning now that she realized she was finally going to have to face her fears. “Do you want me to go in first?” Alex offered, noticing her sister starting to grow pale as they reached the front door.

“No… Let’s just rip the bandage off.” With a nod, Alex moved forward to push the door open, stepping into the black and white front room. There were servants moving between rooms, all glancing to Alex and Kara as they stepped into their old home.

One of the older maids that the two sisters had grown up with froze when she saw them, dropping the stack of books she was holding. They clattered to the ground as she stared at the rugged women in front of her.

Suddenly the maid gave a blood curdling screech, turning in sprinting up the stairs. “Ghost! There’s a ghost in the house!” Kara stood in shock, watching her former maid literally run away from them.

“We should really find mom, before she does,” Alex pointed out, trying to figure out if she was amused or worried about such a reaction. Kara swallowed hard, silently nodding as she let Alex lead the way upstairs.

They first checked Eliza’s personal office, trying to see if she was working. Unfortunately, the room was empty, so they instead went to see if she was in her bedroom. Alex stepped through the door first, giving it a light knock as Kara paused in the doorway.

Alex turned towards a sound to her right, smiling as her eyes focused on something that Kara couldn’t see. “What? Alex, how are you here? Why is Jodie yelling about a ghost?” Eliza’s voice sounded from where Alex was looking, sending a wave of tingling warmth through Kara’s chest.

She hadn’t seen her mom in almost eleven months, so when Alex silently beckoned for her to walk into the room, it was hard to not immediately run to her mother and hug her when she stepped into view.

Despite not being biologically related, Eliza had long, blonde hair just like Kara, although there was now a considerable amount of grey at the roots. She had the Danvers jaw, and her eyebrows were lowered just like Kara and Alex’s did when they were confused.

She was considerably thinner than last time Kara saw her, even in her deep blue and green gown she was wearing. Her eyes were darker than Kara’s, mirroring the color of the sea before a storm, rather than the sky.

When Eliza’s eyes landed on her once dead daughter, her face went as pale as paper. She was sitting at her vanity, mid-way through putting on earrings. She stared at Kara through the mirror she had been looking through, visibly trying to figure out if she was really a ghost or not.

Swallowing hard, Kara meekly mumbled, “Hi, mom…”

The sound of her youngest daughter’s voice seemed to make Eliza realize she wasn’t imagining her. Slowly, she turned around on the stool she was sitting on to stare directly at Kara. Her dark blue eyes started to grow wet with tears as she stood.

Anxiously clenching and unclenching her jaw, Kara silently stood still while Eliza carefully walked up to her. She couldn’t help but notice how much older her mother’s hands looked when she reached up to grab her warm cheeks, gasping when her hand’s didn’t pass right through her.

“You’re… You’re here… How?” Eliza’s voice was barely a whisper, her eyes wide as one tear rolled down her cheek, then another. Kara frowned at the tears, her eyebrows knitting together with emotion. “It’s a really long story, but… God, I missed you.”

Both women surged together into a big hug, the force of the embrace so hard that it almost knocked the wind out of Kara.

“You’re back! My baby’s back- both of them!” Eliza sobbed, reaching for Alex to join the hug. All three Danvers hugged tightly, everyone but Alex crying. Eliza kept whispering that Kara was back, and how she thought she was dead.

“I’m here, mom… I’m not gone, I swear,” Kara whispered into golden curls. “How did you get here? Both of you?” Eliza allowed her daughters to pull away, sniffling as she wiped at her cheeks.

“It really is a long story, mom,” Alex agreed, glancing to Kara, “But we’re here for you… We want to take you somewhere; to our home…”

“We want to be a family again, mom,” Kara added, hoping that Eliza would actually listen to them.

The frown on her face, however, showed her reluctance. “Home?... But this is your home!”

“This house might be, yeah,” Alex matched her mother’s frown, furrowing her eyebrows in slight frustration. “But this land isn’t, mom… We’ve found a new place; new people that we’re attached to, and care about. There’s so much we want to show you,” Kara finished.

“I don’t- I don’t know what you two are talking about… Where is this place? I’m so confused, I thought you’d come back here to stay, Alex…”

“I can’t, mom… Not after what happened to us; I refuse to serve under another unjust ruler-” Alex defended, visibly showing her frustration, like they’d had this conversation a hundred times before.

“It’s an island,” Kara interjected, wondering if they explained it a little more, then Eliza wouldn’t be so reluctant, “Full of free and wonderful people. It’s where I’ve been, all this time.”

Eliza’s eyebrows lowered as she crossed her arms across her chest, hugging herself. “Where you’ve been all this time?”

Realizing that her mother wouldn’t budge, unless she had a better understanding of what happened to her daughter, and where she went, Kara launched into her story. She sugar-coated it much more compared to when she told Alex about it, leaving all the gruesome parts out.

She didn’t want to lie to Eliza, but if she knew her past with Lena, she was afraid she’d never go with them. Kara had originally wanted to bring Lena along with them, but not only did she have duties as ruler of National City, but she also didn’t want to risk getting in trouble with the law if anyone figured out who she was.

Eliza listened with a deep frown on her face the entire time, visibly trying to take in all the information she was just told. “Girls, I… What about this place? Our home?” She gestured to the house, not wanting to just abandon the home she’d lived in for decades.

“You could always sell it,” Alex offered with a shrug. “This house has been in your father’s family for generations,” Eliza countered. “Then we keep it, and give it to another Danvers relative,” Kara suggested.

Their mother still seemed reluctant as she glanced around the room, a troubled look on her face. “Mom,” Kara softly said, stepping forward to take her mother’s hands, “We know it’s scary, and I understand if you want to keep the house and stay here… But you’ve always been the one to say that family is the most important thing in the world. We want to keep our family together; in a better, safer place.”

Dark blue eyes meet lighter blue as mother and daughter stared at one another. Kara’s reminder of Eliza’s words seemed to calm her anxiety and reluctance, her face softening. “I do say that, yeah… There’s just so many memories in this house…”

“And we’ll build new memories in this new place,” Alex spoke next to them, moving to put a hand on Eliza’s shoulder. She looked at her daughters for a long moment, eventually sighing heavily. “You’re right… I’d rather be with you, than alone with this empty house.”

Relief flooded through both Kara and Alex at their mom’s words, realizing she had finally un-dug her heels. Kara could have burst into tears all over again at the words. “Thank you, mom,” she whispered, pulling Eliza into another hug.

Her mother hummed softly into the hug, squeezing her daughter tightly. “We’ll help you get everything arranged; I’ve got a ship, and a crew that can help us if needed,” Kara mentioned, figuring it’d take them a few days to get everything situated.

In fact, it took about a week for Eliza to get the estate settled- she wrote to both the bank, and some close relatives that she was moving away, and would be giving the house to them and their growing family –as well as pack up all her personal things.

For the most part, she left a lot of the furniture and decorations around the house, but there were some items she refused to part with. For example, a trunk with an intricate lock that Alex’s father had given to her as an anniversary gift.

With The Oryx’s Revenge’s crew, they managed to load up all of Eliza’s things onto the ship. The very last thing they did before leaving was withdrew all of Eliza’s money from the bank. They tried to question her on why she needed to empty the account, but after Alex threatened to complain that their questioning was impeding their rights to their hard earned money, they finally got what they went there for.

On the morning of the seventh day, the Danvers family finally boarded Kara’s ship, and left Midvale. Eliza watched the town grow smaller and smaller from the back of the ship, a mixture of emotion on her face. “We can always go back and visit, if that’s what you’re worried about,” Kara suggested as she stepped away from the helm.

“No, it’s okay; I just needed a moment to say good-bye.” Eliza turned and smiled to her daughter, the early morning sun making her look much younger. “We’ll be happy together in National City, I promise,” Kara smiled back. Eliza reached over and ran her fingers lovingly through Kara’s loose hair under her captain’s hat.

“As long as I have you and Alex, I’ll always be happy.”

It took another two weeks to get from Midvale to National City; without Lena’s ability to control the currents, it took a much longer time to get from place to place. Kara led the way on Enzo, while Alex and Eliza followed on their own horses. By now the youngest Danvers knew the island well, confidently making her way to the city.

Just like everyone else, Eliza had the same reaction as Kara and Alex when she saw National City. Her eyes were wide, and her mouth was ajar as she stared up at the castle as they walked through the city. She made the same, “You seriously live here,” comment that Alex did, making Kara laugh with joy.

“It’s been my home for a year now… Lena and Sam should be inside,” Kara smiled, noticing that Alex perked up at the mention of Sam. They’d talked a little more about Alex’s anxieties on the way home, and had determined that she’d finally give in to her desire for them to be together.

Kara herself was excited to see her lover; it’d been over a month of traveling at this point, and she was ready to have Lena back in her arms again.

They stabled their horses as usual, Kara quickly leading the way into the castle. She’d written to Lena when they got to the island that they’d be home shortly, but with their traveling Lena wasn’t able to write back to them.

Kara managed to ask some people passing by where her girlfriend would be, thanking them when they said she was currently in the throne room holding a meeting with some new citizens of National City.

Excitement buzzed in her chest as she quickly led her mom and sister up to the throne room, trying her best not to burst through the doors when they reached them.

Lena was sitting on the old throne that she almost never used, talking to a large group of people about the rules of National City. When her green eyes landed on Kara, who quietly stood in the back of the room with her family, the stoic look on her face broke for a split second into a look of joy.

She couldn’t stop the smile that pulled at her lips, her voice turning from authoritative to excited in a split second. Lena lost her train of thought for a moment, tearing her eyes away from her lover to try and remember what she had just been saying.

Quickly regaining her composure, she hurried through the last bit of her meeting before dismissing the new citizens. As the crowd left, Kara couldn’t help but quickly walk around them to meet Lena at the front of the room.

They collided into a tight embrace, Kara deeply kissing her lover. It felt indescribable to feel their lips pressed together after five weeks of being apart, smiling wide into the kiss. She almost didn’t want to pull away, but Alex loudly cleared her throat to get their attention.

Reluctantly letting Lena go, Kara stepped back and turned to gesture to Eliza. “Lena, this is my mom,” she introduced, her hand resting on the small of Lena’s back. Her girlfriend smiled wide, moving forward to offer Eliza her hand. “Of course! It’s so nice to finally meet you, Mrs. Danvers; when I heard that you agreed to join us here, I got very excited. I hope your journey went well?”

Eliza beamed at the politeness, reaching forward to happily shake Lena’s hand. “Please, call me Eliza… And from the way Kara and Alex spoke about your wonderful island, it was hard to decline the offer. The seas were kind, thankfully, so the journey wasn’t as hard on my old bones.” She looked to Kara, an approving sparkle in her eyes.

Seeing all the people- save for Sam –that she cared about in one room made Kara’s heart swell with joy. Her cheeks burned with how wide she was smiling, buzzing with happiness. She’d never thought she’d be able to see this in a million years, but her dream finally came true.

“I’m glad you agreed, I know it’s a big change. Kara’s told me so much about you; she really missed you.” Lena glanced to her lover, her own happiness rolling off of her in vibrant waves.

“I’d love to get to know you a little better,” Lena added, “but I’m sure you’d also like to get off your feet for a little bit, and rest. Let me show you to your room for the night; I’ve set up a house down in the city for you, but it’ll take Kara’s crew a few hours to get it truly ready.”

Eliza visibly sagged at the mention of getting off of her feet, looking exhausted. “A nap sounds like heaven right now; I’m too old to be traveling this much,” she chuckled. Lena moved to start guiding the way to Eliza’s temporary room, but paused when Alex spoke.

“Is Sam here today?” She asked, having been searching the room for her the entire time. Lena gave the elder Danvers sister a knowing smile, having probably had the same conversation Kara and Alex had with Sam

“She’s currently coming home from an emergency meeting a town over, but she’ll be back by dinner tonight. She said that’s where she’d like to meet you, if you’re ready.”

Alex looked a little deflated that she couldn’t see Sam at that moment, but the idea of still being able to see her later seemed to keep her hopes up. “Alright, thank you… I’m going home to wash up.” She gave Eliza a tight hug, waving to Kara and Lena before leaving the room.

Lena led the way to Eliza’s room; a spare room among the many guest bedrooms in the castle. It had been cleaned up and lightly furnished, with the windows pushed open to let the fresh mountain air in. “Dinner will be around six, if you’re hungry, Eliza… I’ll leave you two to it?”

She moved to step out of the room, but Eliza quickly spoke up. “Kara, go spend some time with your girlfriend; I’ll be fine here. I’m just going to rest for a bit.” Her mother gave Kara an approving look, smiling and winking to her.

Smiling wide, Kara gave Eliza a quick kiss on the cheek before leaving the room with Lena. Instantly she laced their fingers together, swinging their hands as they walked. “Hey, you,” she hummed, chuckling at Lena’s bright, Kara-only smile.

“How was your trip?” she asked casually, happily listening to Kara explain everything that went down in the past five weeks. They went up to Lena’s bedroom, sitting together as Lena also explained what happened in National City.

They talked, kissed, and cuddled for the next few hours, soaking up the other’s presents. Kara made sure to study Lena’s beautiful face all over again, even though she had already memorized it months ago. She happily traced her thumb all around Lena’s smiling lips and broad jaw as they cuddled, humming in satisfaction.

Eventually the decided to make their way down to the banquet hall, grabbing Eliza on the way. Kara’s mother chatted happily to Lena, already talking to her about Kara’s old home in Midvale, and embarrassing childhood stories.

Alex was in the banquet hall when the trio walked in, noticeably anxious as she tried to eat a bread roll. Kara flounced into the seat next to Alex, smiling to her. “So, you going to do it?” she quietly questioned, poking at her sister’s ribs.

Flinching away from the touch, Alex shot her a half-glare. “I said I would, didn’t I?”

“Yeah, well you look like you’re about to throw up and back out of the plan, so I was just making sure,” Kara laughed, knowing her sister wasn’t being purposefully abrasive. Eliza leaned forward from where she sat to look at Alex. “Oh, you’re going to ask this Sam of yours out?”

Alex’s face turned bright red, opening her mouth to say something defensive, but froze when she looked across the room to see that Sam had just entered. Snapping her jaw just with a loud ’click’, Alex sat back in her seat and wiped her sweaty palms on her pants.

Sam also paused, staring at Alex with her own anxious look. Awkwardly, Alex pushed her seat back and stumbled around it. “Go get ‘em, tiger,” Kara joked, Lena scoffing next to her. “You guys are so mean,” she scolded playfully, watching Alex shuffling around the table to go meet Sam.

“Yeah, but it’s funny,” Kara joked back, leaning forward onto her elbows as she watched Alex walk up to Sam. She said something that no one could hear because of the distance, gesturing to the door. Sam glanced past her to Lena before giving a nod, and leading the way out of the room.

“Damn!” Kara huffed, realizing she wouldn’t get to see her sister finally facing her fears. Quickly she got up, moving to sneak through the back door. “What are you doing?” Lena questioned, arching her eyebrow. “I’m gonna listen from around the corner!”

Eliza also stood, giving Lena a ‘why not?’ shrug as she hurried to follow her daughter. With a groan, Lena followed as well, jokingly mumbling how bad of a person Kara was for eaves dropping.

Quietly moving down the hallway, Kara peaked her head around the corner, spotting Sam and Alex a few yards away. Alex was talking low, and Sam was hugging herself with a nervous look on her face.

Unfortunately, Kara wasn’t able to hear exactly what Alex was saying due to her low voice, but she could tell that she was stumbling over her words, and was starting to get frustrated with herself.

After multiple false-starts, she seemed to forego her idea of talking, instead grabbing Sam’s cheeks and roughly pulling her into a very heated kiss. Even from this distance Kara could hear Sam’s gasp, watching her hurriedly uncross her arms and grip the front of Alex’s shirt.

The kiss was firm and passionate as her sister started to push backwards until Sam was pressed against a wall, her hands moving to her shoulders. Realizing that the kiss was going someplace that Kara did not want to see, she quickly backed away from the scene.

She looked back at her mother and Lena, eyes wide in surprise. Eliza also peaked around to see what was going on, quickly looking away.

“Oh, uh… Good for her?” She mumbled in shock, making Lena and Kara snort.

**********

Kara ducked under a branch as Enzo followed Outlaw through the woods. Lena led the way to their favorite get away spot; the waterfall.

It’d been two years since they’d gotten together officially, and so they decided to celebrate where it all started. They had also done this last year, but this time Lena was being super secretive with her anniversary gift.

Last year she had at least given Kara a hint about what she was getting- a beautiful portrait of them together, as well as a beautiful necklace –but this year she refused to say anything about the gift.

“Lenaaa,” Kara complained from where she sat on Enzo, brushing away another branch, “Can’t you just give me one tiny hint?!” Her curiosity was killing her; she wanted to know what her brilliant yet mysterious girlfriend had in mind for her.

Lena glanced back at her lover with a smirk, silently shaking her head before turning back forward. “We’re almost there, then you’ll see,” she said in an amused voice. Kara groaned loudly, but her pouting didn’t get her anywhere with Lena’s iron strong resolve.

They arrived to the waterfall a little while later, but Lena didn’t get off of Outlaw straight away. “My gift comes in two parts,” she said, turning to Kara, “and the first part is a little too big to be here.” Kara furrowed her eyebrows, confused by what Lena meant by that.

“Here- follow me; it’s just a half mile further.” She kicked Outlaw back into a walk, crossing the shallow river and back into the forest. Kara hadn’t noticed until now, but there seemed to be a fresh path that was made through the other half of the woods, leading into the trees.

Hundreds of questions flooded Kara’s mind, but before she could even figure out which one she wanted to ask first, Lena stopped Outlaw in the middle of the path. “Let’s go on foot; it’ll make the surprise even better.”

She slid off of her black stallion, Kara following suit. “Where are you taking me?” She asked, catching up to Lena as she continued to move down the path. “You’ll see, Sweetheart,” Lena smiled, flashing her dimples that Kara loved to kiss.

Huffing at her lover’s unsatisfactory answer, she finally went quiet, trusting Lena’s reluctances to give her a straight answer.

They only had to walk for a few minutes before the wide path opened up into a large clearing full of flowers.

But it wasn’t just full of flowers.

The skeleton of a large, homely house sat in the center of the clearing, seemingly having just been started. There was construction equipment and piles of lumber all around the house, although there seemed to be no workers this late in the afternoon.

Kara paused as she looked at the house, eyebrows raising to her hairline as she stared at the beautiful home. “What… What’s this?” she questioned, tearing her eyes away from the cottage to look at her lover, who was watching her reaction.

“A home away from home… A vacation house, for when we don’t want to deal with the noise of the city, and just want to get away together. We’ve camped at the falls a few times before, but I figured this would be more comfortable.”

Lena gave her lover a testing smile, still searching her face for some sort of reaction. Kara looked back at the cottage, her chest swelling with love and joy. This was all she could ever hope for; a place her and Lena could truly call theirs.

While they did run National City together, it was clear that Lena was the true ruler of the castle. However, here- away from the city and its people –they could really share the space. It was in a beautiful part of the woods, right next to a spot that had a ton of sentimental value.

“Lena- wow… Baby, I love it!” Kara gasped, her excitement pouring out of her in waves. She turned and grabbed her girlfriend’s hips, pulling her into a passionate kiss. As she did a hundred times before, Kara poured all her love into the kiss, humming as she felt Lena smile against her lips.

She pulled slightly away after a moment, resting her forehead against Kara’s. “You really love it?” She asked, green eyes looking into blue. “With all my heart,” Kara whispered, drawing an ‘X’ on her scarred chest. Lena smiled at the reassurance, moving to step back.

“Come look inside; I’ll show you all the rooms.” She offered her hand, which Kara instantly took. They walked into the house, which was still only a foundation and the frame work. The walls just looked like pieces of wood sticking up into the air, but as Lena moved around and started to point out specific areas, Kara could really see it coming together.

“This would be the kitchen,” Lena pointed to a room with stone floors instead of wood, “And this would be the dining room,” It was right next to the kitchen, with what looked to be a window pointing out into the back yard, “Here’s the living room, bathroom, and spare bedroom.”

As Kara looked around, she noticed some stairs that led up to a non-existent top floor. “Oh- here’s where the master bedroom will be,” Lena explained, pointing above their heads. She continued to show what the second story would look like, mentioning that they would also have a basement for storage.

By the end of the tour Kara could perfectly picture the house. She was smiling wide, looking at their future vacation house. She stepped out onto the front porch, looking into the front door while Lena stood next to her.

“This is one of the most beautiful things you could have ever done,” Kara whispered, her voice dripping with emotion. She wanted to cry, feeling that familiar burning just behind her eyes. “I’m not done just yet,” Lena hummed softly, gently grabbing Kara to turn her so they faced one another.

“You seriously have more?!” Kara gasped, unsure on how she could possibly have another gift. Lena gave a small shrug, smiling softly. She visibly started to get a little nervous, reaching to hook her thumbs in her back pockets.

“Yeah, but first I’d like to say something,” she half mumbled, shifting her stance slightly. Confused by her lover’s sudden anxiety, Kara reached forward and rested a hand on her lover’s waste. “I’m listening, Goddess,” she whispered, rubbing her thumb against Lena’s ribs reassuringly.

She took a deep breath, looking Kara in the eyes as she broke into her speech. “I love you more than anything in this universe… We’ve been together for a while now, and have gone through a lot of stuff that could have taken one- or both –of us away from the other…”

Kara listened silently, trying to figure out where this loving speech was going.

“…You have no idea how thankful I am that you’re in my life, and that I cherish every day you’re with me,” Lena glanced down to where Kara’s scar was hidden under her shirt. She reached forward to gently touch it. “You’ve sacrificed so much for me, and that makes me love you so much more.”

Kara smiled softly, humming at the contact as it sent a wave of warmth rolling down her spine.

“When you suggested we go kill Victoria, I was about to ask you to marry me right then and there, but I figured I should wait till after the battle… But with everything that happened- your sister joining us, then your mom agreeing to move over as well, I decided to put my idea on the back burner.”

Kara furrowed her eyebrows, silently wondering if Lena was going to do what she thought she was going to do.

“A lot has been going on the past two years, but now that everything’s as situated as it can be, I was able to have a conversation with Eliza… And she gave me the permission I needed to go and do this…”

Lena stepped away from Kara, reaching into her front pocket to pull something out. As she did, she knelt down, looking up to her lover. “I’m certain that I want to spend the rest of my life with you,” Lena held up a small ring; a silver band with a Celtic knot design and little emerald stones set between each knot, “And I hope you are as well… Kara Danvers, will you-“

“Yes! Yes, yes, yes! I’ll marry you- yes!” Kara dropped onto her knees, wrapping a hand around the ring as she passionately kissed Lena. She could feel her lover physically relax against her, all the tense anxiety leaving her body as she leaned further into the kiss.

Kara pulled away, turning to offer her left hand to Lena. The ring fit perfectly on her finger, dazzling in the late afternoon light. Kara stared at it in awe, studying the beautiful design. “Oh my god, I love it- I love you!”

Again they kissed, Kara grabbing Lena’s face with both hands. Her fiancé smiled against her lips, mumbling her own, “I love you,” as well.

After multiple minutes, Lena finally got Kara to let her face go, both of them laughing in their shared space. “Now my present’s not going to seem as cool,” Kara realized through her laughter, which only made her laugh more.

“I’m certain I’ll still love it- plus you already gave me the gift of saying yes to being my wife,” Lena smiled wide, her eyes soft and full of love. Kara mirrored the smile, pecking her on the lips one more time before helping her stand from their kneeling position.

Whistling for Enzo, Kara hurried to her saddle bags to grab her gift to Lena. She trotted back over once retrieving the wrapped item, handing the small box to her. “Go ahead, open it.”

Lena thanked her softly, carefully pulling the paper off of the gift. It was a book that Lena had mentioned wanting a few months ago; a sequel to her favorite story, which had only been released a few weeks ago. Kara had managed to ask one of the traveling crews that helped supply the island to get the book for her, and it had arrived a few days ago.

“Oh my god, Kara! I love it!” Lena gasped, recognizing the name of the book. She went to hug her fiancé, but Kara stopped her. “Wait, there’s a second thing; it’s inside the book, here.” She pointed out that there was something small hidden inside the center of the book.

Opening it up, Lena could see that it was a beautifully woven book mark; something that she always complained about not having, talking about how she hated to dog-ear the pages. Lena’s eyes went wide at the gift, carefully picking it up.

“I made it myself,” Kara quietly added, glancing between the gift and her lover’s face. Lena looked up in surprise, then confusion. “When did you have time?” Kara smiled at the question, chuckling softly. “In the mornings and evenings, when you were busy or asleep.”

That Kara-only smile that made her melt split across Lena’s face as she stepped forward to hug and kiss her. “This is amazing, Sweetheart; I couldn’t have asked for anything better.”

Kara tightened her arms around Lena, kissing the side of her head. “I love you,” she whispered, glancing at the ring on her finger with a dazzling smile.

“I love you more,” Lena hummed back with a chuckle.

**********

Kara stood in the field of flowers anxiously, trying not to pull at her pants.

She wore a suit; tailcoat buttoned across her chest, high wasted pants tucked into brand new boots, a vest and high-collared shirt under the matching coat. Her hair was pulled back into a bun, the front bumped up while the actual bun was twisted into an intricate swirl.

Eliza stood next to her in a dress; a deep golden gown with beautiful black designs embroidered into the bodice. Alex was on her other side, wearing a similar dress, but in a hunter green color. Tifa La Mode had designed them for the wedding, made specifically to match yet set apart.

They were standing in front of Kara and Lena’s cottage, silently waiting for the music to start; a que for Sam and Lena to walk out. The entire field was empty, save for the small string quartet, and the Danvers. Lena had asked Kara if they could keep the wedding small, but celebrate in the city, when they were officially married.

Of course Kara agreed, figuring it’d be both safer, and easier to be away from National City. She couldn’t even imagine how hard it would be to seat the entire population in one area, and expect them to stay quiet while they mumbled their vows.

So, the best option was to do the reception in a much quieter space; naturally they chose the cottage.

Beside Kara, Eliza shifted into her daughter’s view. “Ready?” She asked with a smile, seeing her nerves clearly written on her face. Unable to unclench her teeth, Kara simply nodded her agreement; she’d been waiting for this day for so long, and it was finally here…

With a nod from Eliza, the band started to play the chosen song; ‘A Rose in Spanish Harlem’, the first song they had ever danced to together. Lena had explained a long time ago that that’s when she started to realize she actually had a heart, and that her feelings for Kara were more than that of captor and captive.

At the sound of the music, there was movement behind the drawn curtains of the cottage. Kara stared at the front door, watching it open with so much tension in her muscles she felt like she was going to pull something.

Sam walked through the door first, smiling to the trio waiting a few yards away; she also wore a dress just like Eliza and Alex’s, but this one was a royal blue. She stepped out onto the porch, and turned to look at the door just as Lena’s figure walked through the doorway.

It felt like everything had frozen in time.

Her hair was a sea of black curls around her shoulders, parted from the side and pulled into a beautiful half-up. Her pale skin was enhanced by deep crimson lipstick and dark make-up, drawing out her enchanting green eyes.

Lena’s dress was burgundy, with a lace bodice that dipped so low in the front that Kara could almost see her belly-button. Around her shoulders was a sort of cape attached to the dress, surrounding her bare arms and reaching all the way to the floor.

Kara stared at her fiancé as she carefully stepped down the stairs, walking across the well-worn path that led straight to where Kara was standing. She held a bouquet of flowers in her hands, the morning sunlight catching her engagement ring- just like Kara’s, but with sapphires instead of emeralds –just right as she walked down towards them.

The two lovers smiled at one another, beaming wide. If Kara had any doubts about her love or decision to marry Lena- she most certainly didn’t –they were all wiped away in that moment.

Seeing how beautiful she was, Kara felt as if she was falling in love all over again.

Lena made her way over to Kara by the end of the song, Kara stepping forward and offering a hand to her fiancé. Handing her flowers off to Sam, who stood opposite to Alex, she moved to grab both of Kara’s hands.

With the brides in place, Eliza stepped next to them, a small notebook in her hand; she’d agreed to officiate their wedding, since there was no law saying she couldn’t. She smiled wide to her daughter and soon to be daughter-in-law, their excitement pooling together.

“If you had told me, almost three decades ago that the little girl in my arms was going to make me the proudest mother in the world… I would have believed you,” Eliza spoke to the group, everyone chuckling at the joke.

“Kara, I can’t tell you how happy you’ve made me, and it makes me even happier seeing you live such a fulfilling life… Lena, we may have not known one another for very long, but I’m proud to call you my daughter. You’ve loved my baby girl more than anyone ever has- and that’s saying something!”

Again, the group chuckled, Kara glancing to Lena with a beaming smile. “It warms my heart knowing I’m able to see you two beautiful ladies getting the happy endings you deserve, and I can’t wait to watch you grow even more together.”

Eliza started to get choked up as she spoke, tears openly dripping down her cheeks. Kara felt her own tears coming forward, taking a second to pull a hand away from Lena’s to gently grab her mother’s arm. She gave her a reassuring squeeze, smiling to her. “Thank you, mom…”

After a few sniffles, Eliza nodded and continued her speech while Kara returned her hand to Lena’s. “I’m so glad the universe brought you two together, and let such a beautiful relationship blossom… Now, let’s get on with the vows; Lena, would you like to go first?”

Lena nodded, taking in a deep, anxious breath. She turned to Sam, dropping one of Kara’s hands to grab the folded paper that was offered to her. She carefully unfolded it, glancing between the vows and Kara as she spoke.

“I’ve already said it a hundred times before that you, Kara, have saved me… You saved me from a life full of anger and misery; saved me from a faulty curse that was ruining the good inside of me… I can’t thank you enough for that, but I can promise to live my life in honor of you…”

She gave Kara’s hand a tight squeeze, pausing to smile, “…You, who managed to make me feel who I once was from day one. You, who made me realize the impossible had happened, and that I had been given my heart back- even if that scared me to death. You, who can make my smile and laugh just by sitting and being yourself.

“When you agreed to marry me, I thought I could never be happier, but I was wrong; this moment, right now, is the happiest I’ve ever been… But to get to the point,” Lena chuckled, “I’m here today, to promise to always love you, and cherish the love you give me. I promise to be by your side, as you’ve been by my side.

“And lastly, I promise to grow old with you, because three years ago I didn’t think I was going to get to grow old… I love you more than life itself, Kara Danvers, and I can’t wait to love you for the rest of time.”

Lena folded her paper, her own eyes watering as she looked up to Kara. Tears poured down her cheeks as she looked to her fiancé, smiling wide at the beautiful vow. Lena softly squeezed her hand, chuckling as Kara brushed her tears away with the back of her hand.

She reached into the inside pocket of her jacket, pulling out her own vows. She unfolded them, trying to blink through the tears to read what she had written.

“Lena Luthor… When I had learned your real name, I couldn’t stop myself from thinking about how beautiful it was… I kept saying it in my head whenever I looked at you; ‘Lena… Lena… Lena’. Even when you would get mad at me for slipping up, and calling you by your real name, I kept insisting on saying it over and over again.”

Kara paused to wipe a tear off of the tip of her nose. “Eventually, you started to trust me enough to keep calling you that, and I’m so happy you did. It was after that moment that our relationship really started to grow into what it is today.

“Your trust in me showed me how much you really cared, and proved to me that you deserve my own trust. Ever since then, I’ve never mistrusted you; you’ve always been there for me, protecting me and showing me what it really means to be free.

“You showed me my new home, my new people. Without you, I’d still be serving under a ruler who didn’t deserve to be where she was. You showed me that this life is worth living, and I can’t wait for you to show me so, so much more.

“I know our relationship started off rocky, but without that, I don’t think I would ever know the true you. I’ve grown to realize that without everything we went through, I wouldn’t be here today. I’m so happy with where we’ve gone, and I’m even happier to see where we’re going.

“I will go with you wherever you take us, because of my trust in you. I will never doubt you, or our relationship; you’ve proven to me through love and promises that there’s no reason to doubt you. I love you with all my being, Lena Luthor, and I’m so excited to see where this new path in our shared lives takes us.”

Now everyone was crying as Kara slipped her vows into her pocket again. She smiled lovingly to Lena, who mirrored the look, laughing a little at how everyone was sniffling and wiping away tears.

After a moment of collection, Eliza was able continue on with the service. “That was beautiful, ladies… So, Lena Luthor; do you vow to take after Kara Danvers, through the good and the bad, in sickness and in health, for all of eternity?”

Lena nodded quickly, instantly agreeing. “I do.”

“And Kara Danvers,” Eliza turned to her daughter, “Do you vow to take after Lena Luthor, through the good and the bad, in sickness and in health, through all of eternity?”

“Yes, of course- I do,” Kara replied.

Eliza looked between the two women, excitement dripping from her words. “Then by my motherly power, I am ecstatic to pronounce you wife and wife; you may kiss!”

Kara took one step forward, closing the distance as soon as the last syllable left her mother’s mouth. She kissed Lena so hard, she was worried all her lipstick would be wiped off.

But her love and joy over the situation made her forget the worry as soon as the thought popped into her head. Lena wrapped her arms around Kara’s neck, holding on tight when she dipped them together.

There were cheers around them, Sam and Alex whooping and hollering together. Kara sat Lena back onto her feet, smiling as wide as physically possible, looking into her wife’s dazzling green eyes.

“I love you, Sweetheart… For all of eternity,” Lena whispered through the cheering, resting her forehead against Kara’s.

“And I love you more, Baby,” Kara whispered back, softly kissing Lena’s nose.

She could practically hear their hearts hammering together.

THE END
